New: tests/test_dmz_records.py (17 tests).
- B-102, the build runner reads a DONE line at the start of the report: check 3 (`check_answer`) passes when the first or the last non-empty line starts with `DONE:`, no line starts with `BLOCKED:` and the report does not end with a question; everything else stays asked. B-074, B-075, B-078 and B-083 ended asked with a commit and a report that began with `DONE: commit ...`. Tests: `tests/test_build_done_first_line.py`.
- B-087, DMZ 3 (the owner's word of 2026-11-25, "Согласен полностью": the checks stay at the entry and at the exit, everything inside runs no content check): no gate inside, the public push keeps names and real secrets. A commit in a project folder (an ask project too), in a build clone and in the working copy of the public repository runs no content check: a name, an IP address, a UUID, a hash, a tenant number, a home path and a key pass (`awb gate --staged` and `--message` in a project or with the new `--inside`; the hooks of this repository and `hooks-build/` pass it). What stays is no content: a co-author line that names an assistant, an item of a project's `OPEN.md` without a date and a text over 8 MiB. A commit of the public repository is checked at its push: `hooks/pre-push`, the runner's push of `main` and the merge message of `awb pr take` (new `awb gate --message FILE --exit`) keep the registered names, secrets, private keys, tokens, home paths and the blocklist and never report an id (32 hex, UUID, tenant, domain or project number). The runner checks no content of a prompt (`awb build add`, `awb build answer`, the prompt of a task) or of a result line (commit subjects, test ids, the end of the suite log) and its check 6 is red for a co-author line or a text over 8 MiB only; the deploy gate needs no register. Tests changed to the new behaviour (names kept): tests/test_gate.py::test_hook_refuses_a_finding_and_lets_a_clean_commit_pass and ::test_the_repository_hooks_refuse_a_commit_message_with_a_finding; tests/test_ask_kind.py::test_the_commit_gate_of_an_ask_project_checks_secrets_only; tests/test_deploy_gate.py::test_the_gate_scans_the_range_and_names_commit_and_class_only and ::test_a_gate_that_cannot_run_refuses and ::test_without_a_working_gate_the_plan_is_refused; tests/test_build_queue.py::test_a_prompt_with_a_planted_value_is_held_by_its_class and ::test_add_refuses_a_planted_form_token_or_home_path_and_writes_nothing and ::test_add_refuses_when_the_name_check_cannot_run and ::test_answer_goes_to_an_asked_task_only_and_passes_the_gate; tests/test_build_runner.py::test_a_subject_with_a_registered_form_is_withheld_from_result_md and ::test_a_prompt_that_fails_the_name_check_is_held_and_starts_once_it_is_clean and ::test_a_name_check_that_cannot_run_after_a_run_is_held_and_reruns and ::test_a_registered_form_in_a_commit_of_the_branch_is_red_gate_before_any_code_runs and ::test_each_selftest_case_fails_when_its_check_is_taken_away[n11-subject] (the check taken away is the exit check of the push, `scan_commits` of the runner); tests/test_seal.py::test_the_build_hooks_run_the_installed_gate_without_the_register. The selftest case n11-subject of `awb/build_selftest.py` now expects `held: push refused` and the subject shown in `result.md`. New: tests/test_dmz_gate.py (13 tests).
- B-086, DMZ 2 (the owner's word of 2026-11-25, "Согласен полностью": the checks stay at the entry and at the exit, everything inside runs no content check): the session hooks check no content. The prompt hook runs no name check and no structured-data check on typed text, a paste, the result of a background agent or a CI event (no data line any more either) and keeps one refusal: a private key block (`BEGIN ... PRIVATE KEY`) or an access key pair (a key id next to its secret, also as `ak=... sk=...`), typed or pasted, because it would leave the host for the model; a check that cannot run never refuses, so a locked vault no longer stops a prompt. The write hook runs no secret mask, no secret refusal and no name refusal (file, folder or file name) on a file written inside a project and no longer rewrites it; the bidi override, the size limit, the writing check of a deliverable and the checks outside a project folder stay. The start and the compaction show SCOPE.md, STATE.md, OPEN.md, the pinned block, the folder name and the reason a project is parked as written (only a bidi override withholds a section), and the stop hook shows draft file names as written. The ledger draft of the stop hook keeps the commit subjects as written and writes without the text screen of the ledger (that screen goes in B-088). The draft wording check (banned words, vendor names in deliverables and mails) stays. Tests: `tests/test_dmz_hooks.py`; the tests of the old inside checks assert the new behaviour under their names.
- `awb dataset build` also leaves out facts on Windows Server 2008 and 2012 (R2), out of Microsoft support, and on Microsoft licence terms (SPLA, License Mobility), on the owner's word of 2026-11-25; facts on current Windows on TCP stay.
- B-080, WinLab runs no content check for good (the owner's word of 2026-11-25: "Goal: remove every content check (name check and secret check) from WinLab for good, in code and tests, not behind a switch."): a command, a put and a get pass with no name check and no secret check, whatever they hold; a get is published after its SHA-256 compare and a kept part after a kill or a signal is compared and published by `awb winlab get WINPATH FILE --recheck` without the machine. The guards of the machine itself stay (its own state, the `{{secret:}}` reference, the plain whole path, the overwrite rule, the disk space check) and so do the secret masking of logs and printed output and every check of the main work laptop (`awb intake`, `awb doc fetch`, the commit gate). The host line `winlab_checks`, `--public-image` of `awb winlab open` with its field in the window record and the broker answer, `awb winlab get --check` and the disk image note are gone; seal/setup.sh writes no `winlab_checks` line even when the old host file has one. Tests of the removed checks assert the new behaviour (same ids).
- The rules of every project say: a time told to the owner is German time (Europe/Berlin) with its zone, never UTC alone; logs and records keep UTC (his word of 2026-11-25).
- `awb dataset build` leaves out every fact whose statement or tag names a third-party product of the new rules/dataset-third-party.txt (Veeam, OPNsense, SonicWall, Meraki and others), on the owner's word of 2026-11-25: the public facts carry TCP only; the facts stay in the knowledge base for the projects, and the build counts them as "about a third-party product".
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-19: `awb kb find` prints the first 20 entries (`--top N`, `--all`) and sends only those through the name check, slice by slice until 20 are shown; it sent every match before, which is nearly the whole base for a broad query. Measured as the work user on a read-only copy of the base (2,289 entries): 34 s and 38 s before, 2.0 s and 2.6 s after; a narrow query 5.4 s before, 2.3 s after. It also fails closed when nothing matches and the name check cannot run.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-18: the commit gate reads a workbook (`.xlsx`) with `awb/xlsxlite.py` `gate_text` and checks it like a text file: sheet names, every row of values and formulas (cells in their columns, so a credential table across two cells is a secret), shared strings, comments, defined names, document properties and every other text and attribute value get every class, on a line of that text; the raw parts (markup, XML comments, part names, archive extras) get the classes of a PDF outside its streams. It stays `opaque` when it cannot be read safely: more than 200 parts, a part over 40 MiB or all over 32 MiB, bytes outside the parts or an archive comment, an encrypted part, a part that is not XML (a macro project, an embedded object, a picture), a macro content type, an external link, a data connection or an external relation other than a web link, a document type declaration; a `.xlsm` stays opaque. A script under `evidence/` of a project (`.py`, `.sh`, `.bash`, `.zsh`, `.ps1`, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.ts`) may name the Linux home of the host's work user; any other home and every other file keep the homepath rule. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_gate_xlsx.py`.
- tcp-ui5o ask 2026-11-24-2: `awb pdf` keeps the figures of an HTML page: an `<img>` of a PNG or JPEG file next to the page (inside the project or scratch folder, no link) or of a data URI is embedded as an image (alpha as a soft mask, palette and 16-bit PNG converted, an interlaced one refused), an inline `<svg>` is drawn as vector paths with its text as selectable text (no markup printed any more), both scaled to the text width at most and kept on one page with the alt text or figcaption as a caption; a heading takes the colour of its style attribute or a style rule; `href="#id"` is a GoTo link to the page of that element. What a figure cannot show is counted in one stderr line. New module `awb/pdffig.py`. Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_pdf_figures.py`.
- Internal mode step 1, how long the owner waits: `awb owner status` and the owner's hourly timer (`awb owner status --record`, run after the put of `awb-report-codes.service`) keep a wait history in `/var/lib/awb-report-codes/waits.json` (a key per item, never its text; first seen, last seen and end in UTC; 60 days). Each item of `awb owner status` ends with `waits N h`, `awb owner status --history CODE` lists the closed and open waits of a project (start, end, hours, where) and `awb board show` has the column "Waits on you" with the oldest open wait, read from the codes-only summary `owner-waits/waits.tsv` of the shared side (the seal makes the folder, 2770). Tests: `tests/test_owner_wait_times.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-16: `awb lab obs link BUCKET KEY [--minutes N]` (and `labclient.obs_link`) prints a time-limited GET link to one object of a bucket the open lab run made, for a reader without a key such as an ECS of the run: the key service signs it in the query string (OBS V2 with `x-obs-security-token`) with a temporary credential made for that link, 60 minutes by default and 12 hours at most, never on a customer tenant or for an exchange or owner bucket; the URL is printed once and the logs keep the bucket, the key hash and the minutes. A public ACL (`x-obs-acl: public-*`, a grant to everyone, an ACL body with Everyone or AllUsers) in a lab call is refused with a line that names `awb lab obs link` (the owner's decision of 2026-11-24). Tests: `tests/test_bytg_obs_link.py`.
- The keep list in an encrypted vault takes a second phrase again: `seal_file` keeps an existing `.gpg` and refused the new list, so every `awb register keep` after the first one answered "the vault daemon refused the request" (found by tcp-ui5o on 2026-11-23); the sealed list of before steps aside for the seal and comes back when it fails. Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_keep_second_seal.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-3: `awb lab --help` names the Workbench's python for a script that imports `awb.labclient` (plain python3 has no awb module); `awb lab run start` said it already. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_lab_interpreter.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-2: `awb kb add` and `amend` let a file name such as `setupapi.dev.log` pass (a web-address hit followed at once by a known file suffix is a file name; a real web address is still refused), and a refusal for a similar entry now comes after the list of similar entries with `nothing was added` as the last line. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_kb_file_names.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-9: `awb dev status [--all]` lists the open development asks of the project of the working folder (`--all` the answered ones too, with their note and release); a session saw an answer only as a new note before. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_dev_status.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-7: `awb gate` with no argument inside a repository checks the staged files as `--staged` does and says so in one line (the project rules say `awb gate` before every commit; the plain call printed only its usage); outside a repository it still asks for an argument. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_gate_plain.py`.
- tcp-s2z5 ask 2026-11-24-2 (owner's word "ему нужен прямой доступ"): an ask project reaches the test tenants directly, read and lab role, writes and lab runs; a customer tenant, a read-only tenant and another project's code stay refused, and no material enters it. Two tests in `tests/test_ask_kind.py` change their asserts on that word.
- tcp-s2z5 ask 2026-11-24-1: an ask project hands a result out with `awb xchg put` (owner's request); material still never enters it (inbox take, xchg list, import and the rest stay refused). Tests: `tests/test_s2z5_ask_put.py`.
- `awb pdf` and `awb docx` write their file group-readable (0664) inside a project folder, so the owner's `awb xchg put` reads it, and private (0600) in the session scratch folder (owner's word 2026-11-24); the old test that pinned 0600 is replaced. Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_docx_h4.py`, `tests/test_pdf.py`.
- tcp-ui5o asks 2026-11-24-4 to -6: `awb docx` keeps h4 to h6 (and #### to ######) as Heading 4 to Heading 6 with their numbering level, the PDF still draws them as h3; `awb xchg put` names a file it cannot read ("FILE cannot be read by this user: give the file group read") instead of "the exchange folder cannot be opened safely". Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_docx_h4.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-22-1: `awb doc fetch` says why it withholds a page, never the value: the class of the first hit and its line (a form of the name register, a secret class, a control character, the check unavailable). Tests: `tests/test_bytg_doc_withheld_reason.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-21-6: a query written into the path (`/v2/cloudimages?name=x`) is refused with a hint where the query goes (`awb cloud call --query K=V`, a lab call `"query"`, labclient `params=`); the list with a query works beside the path on test and customer tenants. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_path_query_hint.py`.
- tcp-ui5o ask 2026-11-23-3: the first `awb egress allow` works: the shared ledger belongs to the work user and the owner may only read it, so the grant failed with "the grants file could not be read or written safely"; the ledger entry now goes through `sudo -n -u WORK awb ledger add`, whose name check still stops the grant. Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_egress_first_grant.py`.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-23-1: `awb doc fetch` reads Debian pages: `www.debian.org` and `wiki.debian.org` are lines of `rules/allow-domains.txt` beside `debian.org`, which redirects every page to them; the host rule stays exact. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_doc_debian.py`.
- tcp-ui5o ask 2026-11-23-2: `awb register keep --from tcp-xxxx --token "name 2"` keeps the value behind a wipe token of the project's import (read from the private report, never typed or shown), so a public term the wipe took for a name comes back with one `awb import tcp-xxxx --redo` instead of a reveal for each edit; the keep list holds for every later intake. Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_keep_from_import.py`.
- tcp-ui5o ask 2026-11-23-1: `awb cloud call --header K=V` sends an extra request header such as `Client-Request-Id` (RFS answered APIGW.0106 without it); the key service refuses a header of the signature or the scope, a broken one and any on OBS. Tests: `tests/test_ui5o_cloud_call_header.py`.
- `awb lab run status` no longer lists an image as live after its delete when the run recorded it under `/v1/cloudimages/ID` (tcp-bytg 2026-11-22-7: two entries of a run stayed live after `DELETE /v2/images/ID` answered 204): `cloudimages` and `images` are one kind for the same id (`labadmin.KIND_SAME`), so the delete of the image or its 404 ends both. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_lab_cloudimages_delete.py`.
- `awb build check` (Q-0031): the host is an x1.2xlarge.4 with 8 vCPU and 32 GB since 2026-11-23, so the suite unit runs eight test files at a time (was four) under `CPUQuota=800%` (was 400%); the other units keep 200%. Tests: `tests/test_suite_parallel.py` (owner's yes of 2026-11-23 for the quota line).

### Fixed
- B-093, key service caller fences (F3, F4 and F8 of the attacker review of 2026-11-26): the material ops (obs, take_owner, take_lab, inbox_find) and a put take the project from the caller of the folder token, so a session whose token names one project and whose request names another is refused; an ask token gets the ask material line whatever code it names; op call runs the ask refusals (no read-only tenant, no login tenant, its own code only) for the folder token of an ask project again; the rate counts 240 calls a minute per caller project and 240 per uid for a call without the token of a granted project (a recipe run signs more than 120 writes a minute), so one looping session no longer starves the others. The awb client now sends the folder token with the exchange ops too. Tests: `tests/test_keys_caller_fence.py`.
- B-091, runner: a run on the login that ends with api_error (the terminal_reason, the subtype or an api_error_status) and 0 input and 0 output tokens is the subscription limit with no text (B-073 runs 1 and 2) and counts as a limit hit with no reset: the task is held as `held: usage limit` and with `build_model = auto` the next pass runs it on the key, instead of "stopped: success" and a retry. A login run with tokens and a key run keep the old path. Tests: `tests/test_buildrun_api_error_limit.py`.
- `awb lab run end` counts an ECS server that answers in status DELETED as removed; the platform shows a deleted server for a while, so the end reported it as still there (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-s2z5-6).
- WinLab checks nothing when the host file says `winlab_checks = off` (the owner's word of 2026-11-25: WinLab only handles images downloaded from the internet): no name check and no secret check on commands, puts and gets; a get keeps its SHA-256 compare, the machine's own guards and the log masking stay; seal/setup.sh keeps the line. Without the line the checks run as before.
- `awb winlab get` in a window opened with `--public-image` takes a binary file that is not a compressed archive (a disk image, a program, a Veeam backup .vbk, .vib or .vbm) with no name and no secret check, only the SHA-256 compare, on the owner's word of 2026-11-25; a text file and an archive keep their checks. The get loads every module before the transfer, so a deploy during it no longer breaks its end (asks 2026-11-25-tcp-bytg-3 and -4).
- tcp-bj3o ask 2026-11-25-tcp-bj3o-2: `awb lab run end` records the id of a created resource from the keys id, log_group_id and log_stream_id of the answer, so an LTS log group and its streams are deleted (streams first); a create that answers 201 with no known id key is listed as "not recorded" with its path and left behind (exit 4). After a delete the end reads the resource again and only a 404 counts as removed: a resource that still answers is "still there after delete" with its id (tried again in the next pass), and an IAM project the platform keeps is named as left behind. Tests in tests/test_lab_end_lts_iam.py
- The key service takes the folder token of an ask project as its caller, so the Playground's lab run start and its calls to the test tenants are no longer refused; an ask project still reaches test tenants only and moves no material (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-s2z5-4).
- `awb side take` treats the result of a side session as a background agent's result: a host name, an address, a phone number or a MAC address of its work passes, a registered name, a mail, bank data, a register or tax number and a secret still withhold it (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-s2z5-3).
- `awb side status` prints a `reason:` line under a failed side session, taken from the error of the client's JSON answer (the client writes it there, not to the error file), or says that the client gave none (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-s2z5-1).
- `awb doc fetch` drops a byte order mark, zero-width marks and the soft hyphen from a page, so the archive help pages of a vendor (Veeam 9.0 to 11) are no longer withheld for a control character; any other control character still withholds the page (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-s2z5-2).
- `awb winlab get` in a window opened with `--public-image` lets a vendor program (ELF or PE, such as the Veeam extract binary) skip the secret check like a disk image; the name check stays and a plain window keeps the secret check (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-bytg-2).
- `awb winlab put` and run no longer take a quoted `-Description` after a bracketed `(ConvertTo-SecureString $pw -AsPlainText -Force)` for a typed password; a quoted literal inside the bracket is still refused (ask 2026-11-25-tcp-bytg-1).
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-24-3: `awb doc fetch --links` lists the links of the page body before those of nav, header, aside and footer, so a long help tree no longer fills the cap of 400 before the body links.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-24-4: `awb doc fetch` shows a public page whose only finding is a secret-like sample value (vendor cmdlet examples) with those lines masked and counted, when they are at most a third of the page; a name or anything left after the masking still withholds the page.
- tcp-bj3o ask 2026-11-24-1: `awb side take NAME --merge` after a plain `awb side take NAME`, and a second or third merge, no longer conflict. The merge commits the result as `notes/side-NAME.md` and leaves `SIDE-RESULT.md` as the main branch has it; the untracked copy of the plain take gives way. A conflict on any other file still aborts.
- tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-24-1: `awb calc` in a side session records into `calc/calc.tsv` of its project under the same lock, so the main session and a side session never give one K id twice.
- `seal/setup.sh` keeps the owner's line `doc_hosts` of `paths.conf` when it rewrites the host file. The deploy of 2026-11-24 dropped it, so `awb doc hosts add` refused until the line was put back by hand.

## Week 47, 2026-11-16 to 2026-11-22
### Added
- Budget fallback of the build runner (owner's yes of 2026-11-22, `awb/buildbudget.py`): a task session on the subscription login that ends on the 5-hour or the weekly limit is `held: usage limit` with the reset the text names (an hour when none), never failed and never nudged. `build_model = auto` in the host file runs on the login and after a limit runs the held task again and the next ones on the model key until the reset, then on the login again; on the key a block's opus becomes sonnet unless its title names a security review. `key_week_cap_usd` (25 unless set) caps the key runs of an ISO week from `total_cost_usd` of each run (its budget when it reports none) in `runs/key-ledger.tsv`; at the cap the queue waits as `key cap`. Each switch (to the key, back to the login, the cap reached) is one event of the new kind `budget` and one mail with the spend of the week; `awb build list` and `awb owner status` name the mode. `seal/setup.sh` takes `auto` (the key copied, the settings without `apiKeyHelper`) and keeps `key_week_cap_usd`, `seal/verify.sh` checks it. Tests: `tests/test_build_budget.py`.
- Contents with page numbers and a page per part in `awb pdf` (tcp-bytg ask 15): a line `<!-- toc -->` in the Markdown (the same comment in HTML) puts a contents table there, each h1 and h2 after it with a dot leader and the page it starts on, laid out in two passes with the page map of the first (again until it settles, at most four); `--h1-page` starts a new page before each level-1 heading that is not at the top of a page already, off by default so other PDFs keep their pages. 6 tests in `tests/test_pdf_contents.py`.
- Push, not poll, phase 2 (Q-0014): the events file gets the kinds `labrun` (`awb lab run start` and `end`, red when a delete failed), `job` (a run of an awb timer unit, from `systemctl list-timers` and `show`, listed after a minute of running or when it ends red), `vault` and `keys` (the daemon locked or down until it is unlocked) and `login` (the build user's Claude login lapsed, from the runner's `held: login missing`, until a run goes on). The owner's minute timer (`awb owner status --write`) writes the last three from what it sees and then mails one push per new event that needs him (a red end, an end that needs him, a lock, a lapsed login, a blocking dev ask answered) through the key service's notify, the path of the 08:00 mail: each event once (its key in `/var/lib/awb-owner-status/events-push.json`, mode 600), at most five a minute, a mail that did not go out tried again for 24 hours, the first tick after the deploy mails nothing old, codes and fixed words only and a text with a name finding never sent. `awb owner status` shows the new kinds in plain words ("the vault locked since 11:04"); the status file and `GET /api/owner/state` carry `ended` (the last 24 hours) beside `running`. Left out: a login that expires soon and the logins of the owner and the work user (no code reads them yet). Tests: `tests/test_events_phase2.py`.
- TCP Facts names its harness: the README subtitle of `awb dataset build` carries one line "A product of the Architect Workbench" with a link to github.com/shimoza/tcp-awb (owner's wish of 2026-11-20); the facts themselves still name no part of the tooling, the check allows this one line only.
- Shared capabilities (owner decision 2026-11-20, after tcp-bytg built its own PDF writer for Cyrillic): `awb pdf FILE --unicode` embeds a TrueType font of the host (Liberation Sans and Mono, else DejaVu; `awb/pdffont.py`, standard library) as a Type0 font with Identity-H, only the glyphs the document uses and a ToUnicode map, so Russian, German umlauts and Latin come out as selectable text that pdftotext gives back as written; without the flag a document keeps the standard fonts and the refusal names `--unicode` and `--replace`. The project rules say that a shared command that lacks something (PDF, office files, a package) gets an `awb dev ask`, never a tool of the project's own or an installed package; development decides what is installed for all. Tests: `tests/test_pdf_unicode.py`.
- `awb egress allow HOST --project CODE --until DATE --why TEXT`, `awb egress allow --list` and `awb egress revoke HOST --project CODE` (`awb/egressgrant.py`): the owner user, without sudo and never from a project folder, grants one host name to the work user for at most 30 days with a reason; the grant goes into `~/.config/awb/egress-grants.tsv` (0600), a line into `egress-grants.log` beside it and a ledger entry (kind tooling, the project code, the reason through the name check; no host). `awb egress load` (root, the timer every 5 minutes) reads the file of the owner of the host file only when it is a private file of the owner, adds the active grants to the allowed set and drops an expired or revoked one at the next load; a host of `seal/egress/ALLOW.md`, a malformed line or one longer than 30 days is left out and counted, and `awb egress status` shows the grants. The filter knows uids, not projects: a grant opens the host to every session of the work user. A lasting host stays a development commit of ALLOW.md. Tests: `tests/test_egress_grants.py`.
- Desk asks, the owner's word stamp and the live release (UXR9, finding 9 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): `awb desk ask "TEXT" [--blocks]` writes a checked ask into `<shared>/notices/desk/` with the mechanics of a dev ask, a desk session sees a new one once at its prompt, `awb desk list` lists the open ones and `awb desk close HANDLE --note TEXT` delivers the note into the project and closes the ask (awb/deskask.py). `--owner-word TEXT` of `awb projects note` and `awb desk close` ends the note with `owner's word: "TEXT", TIME` (awb/ownerword.py); the work rules and the development rules take it as his decision. `awb version live [COMMIT]` prints the live release from `/opt/tcp-awb/DEPLOYED` or the installed source link and whether a commit is live (awb/version.py). 16 tests in tests/test_desk_ask.py and tests/test_version.py
- `awb clean` updates itself and shows its version (the laptop clean module): on a terminal every `awb clean ...` command prints first `awb clean <short commit> (<commit date>)` (the clone's HEAD, or the `rules/VERSION` stamp an HTTPS update now writes when the commit's tree is the checked one) and once what the last quiet update did; `awb clean shell` adds the start of `awb clean update --boot` at the first shell after a WSL boot (once per boot by the boot id, in the background under `timeout 60`, log `~/awb-laptop/update.log`); `awb clean apt-hook [--check]` prints the one sudo command that writes `/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/99awb-clean` (after `apt update`, `awb clean update --quiet` as his user through runuser, never failing apt) and checks it; `awb clean update` prints the version before and after and a refusal over local changes says it stays on its version; `awb clean doctor` ends with how many commits the install is behind the public main (awb/cleanupdate.py). Tests: `tests/test_clean_update_boot.py`.
- Push, not poll, phase 1 (Q-0014, finding 1 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): the long things write their start, ETA and end to one append-only events file `<shared>/build/events.jsonl` (awb/events.py; codes, ids and fixed words only): a build task or check of the runner (ETA the median of the last of its kind, outcome ok, red, stopped or needs-you), `sudo awb deploy` on a sealed host, `awb winlab open` (the window end as ETA, it ends by itself then) and `awb winlab close`, a dev ask written and closed. `awb owner status` opens with "Running now" (item, since, ETA, overdue) and "Ended in the last 24 h" with red ones marked; the owner status file and `GET /api/owner/state` carry `running`. Timers, the vault lock, the logins and the push mail are not in phase 1.
- Counted frictions (Q-0024, section 3 of the UX review of 2026-11-19): the prompt, stop and pre-compact hooks append one line per event to `<shared>/ux/events.tsv` (UTC time, user kind, session kind, project code or `-`, class), never a word of the prompt and no vault call; a prompt gets the class of the first phrase of the new `rules/ux-phrases.tsv` it carries (status question, nudge, repeated rule, bare yes, pasted block) and one that matches nothing writes no line, the stop hook writes `held-draft` or `held-note` when it holds a reply and the pre-compact hook `compaction`. `awb ux note TEXT` adds a checked hand-written line of class `note`, `awb ux report [--since DATE]` prints the count of each class per ISO week with the change against the week before and `awb owner status` ends with a five-line UX block on Mondays. Folder 2775, file 664, each line one append under a lock; 34 tests in `tests/test_ux_events.py`.
- Health strip and Now on both console pages, dev asks and queue on the owner page (Q-0026 and Q-0025, W1 to W4 of the UX review of 2026-11-19). `GET /api/health` of the console data service answers six tiles (vault, key service, build runner with its running check or task, live release with main and what is not deployed, free disk, awb-web on the live release or not) and Now (open build checks and tasks with their start, lab windows with their end and a mark under 60 minutes, a deploy running or pending), from files, `/proc` and two pings, kept 15 seconds, also while the vault is locked; a reader limited to some projects gets 404. `awb owner status --write` adds the open dev asks of `<shared>/notices/dev/` and the open rows of `awb queue` (id, who, next step, age, stale mark) to the status file at most every five minutes, every text through the name check in one call and withheld with its class on a finding; owner-actions serves them as `dev_asks` and `queue` in the answer of `GET /api/owner/state` (no route of their own). Read only: the close and archive buttons with a TOTP code come later. Tests: `tests/test_console_now_health.py`.
- Pinned block of `STATE.md` kept across a compaction (Q-0006, finding 3 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): a `## Pinned` heading with one line each for the reply language (`- Language:`), a standing limit he set (`- Limit:`), a deliverable with its bucket path (`- Deliverable:`) and a running chain (`- Chain:`). The new PreCompact hook `awb hook compact` of the work user hands the Status: and Next: lines and the block to the summary as plain text and the start with source `compact` prints them before every file; both are checked like `STATE.md` and withheld with one line when the check finds anything or cannot run. The stop hook holds a reply whose block has more than 12 lines or 1500 characters, sits below line 40, comes twice or carries an entry of another kind. New projects start with the block, the start of a project without one asks for it. The client settings, the plugin hooks and the managed settings carry the PreCompact entry (`compact`, never the owner's `pre-compact`); five tests that pinned the settings without PreCompact are replaced, not edited (in test_guard, test_seal, test_handover_write, test_projects and test_release2_e2e). Tests: `tests/test_precompact_pinned.py`.
- `awb build stop C-NNN` (Q-0027): a queued check ends `stopped: by_owner` and never runs; a running one gets the same state and its suite unit `awb-task-C-NNN-suite.service` stopped with the runner's `sudo -n systemctl stop`, and the runner ends it `stopped: by_owner`. Replace on queue: a check records its branch name in `ref`, and a new `awb build check` of the same branch ends a still queued check of an older head with `stopped: replaced by C-MMM` (a stale check of an old head cost 30 minutes on 2026-11-19). Tests: `tests/test_build_stop_wait.py`.
- `awb build wait ID [ID...]` (Q-0008): blocks until the first given check or task ends (`--all`: each), reading the state files every 5 seconds, and prints one line per ended one with id, state, time and the path of `result.md`; exit 0 when each printed one passed, 1 when not, 2 after `--timeout SECONDS`. Tests: `tests/test_build_stop_wait.py`.
- `awb tenant billed` pulls daily and hourly Financial Dashboard v2 bills into atomic shared caches with contract pinning and token-safe errors. `awb cost billed` reads tenant totals, project resource costs and hourly windows with separate disk billing, cache age and missing rows. Includes fake HTTP regression tests in `tests/test_billed.py`.

- Solution packages, phase 1 (Q-0019, DESIGN of 2026-11-19): `awb kb pattern add|support|find|list|show|retire` writes pattern cards into `<kb>/patterns/` with the screens of `kb add` and a fingerprint check of the source project's own strings (hashed into `<shared>/patterns/fingerprints/`); support counts projects as salted hashes; cards never reach the dataset. `awb solutions build|status|screen|release` builds `out/vN/` with live price lines, the service check and SOURCES.tsv, prints the staleness counts and releases only through the release screen (export check, codes, fingerprints, support 2), with a selftest that plants a fingerprint. Then S3, S4 and S7: `<shared>/packages.tsv` (one row per package, written by every build, status and release), the stop hook asks a session once per batch for cards when it changed a deliverable whose project tags meet a package, `awb solutions sweep SLUG` scans the projects for the package's terms (codes and counts) and sends a note or a side session, the refresh has a part `packages` and the board a packages line.
- `awb winlab open` extends a window (Q-0009, finding 6 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): open for the project that holds the open window of the machine gives it the new end instead of refusing, never an earlier one than it had, and adds `--public-image` when given; the window of another project is still refused until close. `--until HH:MM` (local host time, today or tomorrow) stands beside `--minutes`, inside the same 1 to 1440 minute limit. Every open leaves a notice `winlab-NAME.CODE.json` in `<shared>/notices/`; the prompt hook of a session of that project warns once in the last 60 minutes after the registry or the broker confirmed the end, and `awb owner status` lists the window as "lab window of NAME for CODE ends in N min" with the open command that extends it. Close is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_winlab_extend.py`.
- `awb dev close --release COMMIT HANDLE...` (Q-0007, finding 4 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): a development ask closes when a commit names its handle (the ask file name without `.md`, `2026-11-19-tcp-q7m4-5`) in its message. The deploy plan lists the open asks a commit of the target names (`asks`, validated by root), and a deploy that ends well runs `awb dev close` as the work user: the note "live in release X" goes into the asking project and the ask gets `"state": "closed"` with the `release`; a failure is one line and never fails the deploy. No manual `--answers` step.

### Changed
- `awb winlab get` of a disk image in a window opened without `--public-image` says so in one line before the transfer (tcp-bytg 2026-11-22: a 10.3 GB image ran for 15 minutes and was then deleted by the secret check): the line names `awb winlab open NAME --minutes N --project CODE --public-image` of the owner and Ctrl-C; the secret check after the transfer stays as decided on 2026-11-20. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_winlab_disk_note.py`.
- `awb winlab get` never deletes a complete transfer for a check that did not run (tcp-bytg ask 11: ten complete disk images were lost when the check of a 22 GB image, 1 to 2.5 h, was cut off): SIGTERM and SIGHUP during a get become one state line, the file stays unpublished as `FILE.awb-part` with its SHA-256 and the progress of the checks, and `--recheck` resumes them without a transfer; the record names the private name of a file under check first, so a get killed outright is found by the next get or `--recheck` and gets its part name back (a complete file replaces a shorter part); a machine that gives no SHA-256 keeps the part instead of deleting it; a check over 64 MB prints a progress line every minute. A real SHA-256 mismatch, a name or a secret still delete. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_winlab_get_kept.py`.
- `awb lab run status` no longer lists a deleted image as live (tcp-bytg ask 5: image 7d3ddb1a of a run was listed twice after a delete that answered 204): the upload of an image's data (`PUT /v2/images/{id}/file`, `PUT /v1/cloudimages/{id}/upload`) and any PUT below a resource the run made are no create of their own (an object of a bucket still is); a delete that answers 2xx or 404 removes a record that names the same kind and id by another API version (`/v1.1/images/ID` for `/v2/images/ID`), what was recorded below the deleted resource and a live or failed leftover of an earlier run of the project on the tenant (`removed_in` names the run). Tests: `tests/test_bytg_lab_image_delete.py`.
- Every ECS of a lab run carries the tag `awb-project` (tcp-bytg ask 4: a POST of an ECS went through the lab client without it): an ECS of the native API (`POST /v2.1/{project_id}/servers` or `/v2/...`, whose body takes metadata only) gets the ECS tag call `POST /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{id}/tags/action` right after the create, for every caller of the key service; in a lab run a create whose body carries `awb-project` of another project (the tag field of its service, for an ECS also the older `server.tags`) is refused, and an ECS create whose body cannot take the tag is refused before anything is sent; an action on an ECS passes as before. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_lab_ecs_tag.py`.
- Disk images stay out of git in every project (tcp-bytg ask 8: `git add` began hashing a 9.6 GB VHDX under evidence/ and had to be stopped): a spawned project gets the lines `*.vhdx`, `*.vhd`, `*.qcow2`, `*.vmdk`, `*.img`, `*.iso`, `*.raw` and `*.ova` (any case) and `*.awb-part` in its `.git/info/exclude`, which git reads like a `.gitignore` but never tracks, and `awb projects sync` adds the lines an older project lacks and says so per project, without a commit. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_image_ignore.py`.
- One file-safety module (Q-0023, finding R4 of the UX review of 2026-11-19): `awb/safefile.py` holds the pinned read (`open_regular`, `read_fd`, `read_regular`: no link followed, no wait on a named pipe, a regular file, of this uid and with one name where the caller asks, a cap), the atomic write (`write_atomic`: a temporary file beside the target, fsync, rename or link) and the private and shared publish (`publish_private` 600, `publish_shared` 664). The copies in build, cleanupdate, egress, gate, images, intake/keep, kb, labclient, projects, review, rulesync, tcp/tenants, toolguard, vault and winlab call it and keep their modes, temporary names, fsyncs and errors as parameters. One table of modes per path class (`MODES`: private 600, private-dir 700, shared 664, shared-dir 2775) and the folders of tcp-shared both users write (`SHARED_TREE`: recheck and ux, whose mode constants now come from it); `seal/verify.sh` reads it with `python3 -I awb/safefile.py table` and checks that each such folder carries at least 2775 and each file in it at least 664. Tests: `tests/test_safefile.py` (a true two-uid read runs only as root).
- `awb pdf` embeds the host font by default (owner's yes of 2026-11-20): a document with text outside WinAnsi (Cyrillic) embeds Liberation or DejaVu without `--unicode` and reads back as written, where it was refused before; a document all in WinAnsi keeps the standard fonts. `--unicode` is still taken and changes nothing on its own. `--replace` alone keeps the standard fonts and writes `?`, with `--unicode` it embeds and replaces only what the font lacks. No font file on the host is exit 2 with a line that names `--replace`. Two tests that asserted the refusal are replaced on the owner's yes: `tests/test_pdf.py` and `tests/test_pdf_unicode.py`.
- test-10503 is a lab tenant like test-10491 (tcp-ui5o, the owner's decision of 2026-11-20): `labadmin.ALLOWED` is the closed list test-10491, test-10497 and test-10503, so `awb lab run start --tenant test-10503`, `awb lab obs` and keep take it and keys.conf takes `lab_admin = test-10491 test-10497 test-10503`; any other alias is refused as before. The refusals and `awb keys unlock` name the tenants from that one list; the lab run rule of the project template and the work rules name test-10503 and `awb projects sync` gives an older project's rule line the third tenant (rulesync.CLAUDE_CUT). Tests: `tests/test_lab_tenant_10503.py`, no row replaced.
- winlab is offered to a Windows migration only (owner rule of 2026-11-20): a spawned project whose tags or goal name Windows and a migration (the tags `windows` and `migration` of rules/tags.txt, or the words of the goal) gets one sentence about the lab machines on the kind line of its CLAUDE.md; every other project gets none, the work rules say "winlab: only for a Windows migration test; do not offer it otherwise." and the `awb` help names winlab "for Windows migration tests only". A window the owner opened for a project still brings its warnings and `awb winlab list` in a session still says who opens one.
- Close the loops (Q-0007, finding 4 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): the prompt hook shows an ask to an owner session only while it is new (24 hours, once per session), an older open ask stays in `awb owner status`, the hand-over and the console; the hand-over lists a check of a branch only when it did not pass and is of the last 48 hours; the build line of the board drops a task or check that is done, stopped or passed 48 hours before the newest row of state.tsv.
- Lab logs pass the commit gate on the first try (Q-0010): the lab log writers (`awb/lablog.py`, used by `awb/labclient.py` and `awb/winlabsession.py`) write the home folder part of a path as `~` when they write (`/home/<user>/x` as `~/x`, `C:\Users\<user>\x` as `~\x`), so the committed log, its full log and its summary carry none; the gate and the short text rules of a commit message no longer read a fact id (`KB-Q7M4`) or a file id (`F-Q7M4.1`, `codes.OWN_ID_RE`) as a name or a candidate, also when its letters spell a registered acronym; the commit-msg hook of a project (`awb gate --install`) takes a `Co-Authored-By:` line naming Claude and a "Generated with Claude Code" line out of the message itself (a short awk step before `awb gate --message`, so it works with whatever awb is installed) with one stderr line and lets the commit pass, while every other finding still refuses it; `awb gate --message FILE --strip-attribution` does the same in the gate. A project made before this keeps its old hook until `awb gate --install --force --repo FOLDER`. Tests: `tests/test_lab_logs_pass_gate.py`.
- Hook notes in one footer line (Q-0005, finding 2 of the UX review of 2026-11-18): the session start of a project carries the rule that every note of the hooks (a notice, a release line, a receipt, a request, the reason a reply was held) goes into one footer line under the answer, `Notes: ` and the notes joined with `; `, never a paragraph of its own and never above the answer; the first note of a held stop starts with the same shape, and a draft gate line alone keeps its text. A prompt that is an interim notification (it starts with `<task-notification` or `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION` or carries `<side-sessions-event`) gets no note, only the data count line it had, and a stop after such a turn adds none and drafts no ledger entry; the notes wait for the next prompt or stop. Every check, block and refusal is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_hook_notes_footer.py`.
- Large lab logs leave git (Q-0021): a lab run log (`notes/lab-runs/<run>.log`) or a Windows lab log (`notes/winlab/`) that grows over 2 MiB moves to `full/` beside it (the writer adds it to the project's `.gitignore`) and git keeps `<name>.summary.md` with start, end, calls, errors, created resources, the path of the full log and its SHA-256 (written again at the end of the run, `awb/lablog.py`). The commit gate refuses a text file over 8 MiB at once as `too-large` with a one-line hint on this rule instead of scanning it for many minutes (a 105 MB log of tcp-bytg held a commit 8 to 25 minutes); a lab log under the limit keeps the secret-only check.
- Owner rules of 2026-11-17: drafted texts use TCP always and keep his choice of I or we. Remove pronoun checks and the I-rate metric from writing and translation. Replace the affected tests and update the test registry.
- `awb winlab open --public-image` (owner): his statement that the disk images of the window are public images built in the lab; a get of a disk image or a compressed file in that window skips the secret check, whose patterns hit the noise of image bytes, and says so in the log. The name check of a compressed file and every check of a plain file stay.
- `awb pdf FILE.md` draws a Markdown pipe table as a ruled grid: a bold header row on a light shade, repeated at the top of every page the table runs onto, column widths fitted to the page width from the content, text wrapped inside its cell with no wrap arrow and left, centre or right alignment from the separator row (`:--`, `:--:`, `--:`). HTML tables and the tables of the dataset HOW-TO stay aligned Courier text. Tests: `tests/test_pdf_tables.py`.
- `awb build check` (Q-0013): the suite unit runs the whole suite as one pytest per test file, four at a time from one queue, each worker with a HOME and a TMPDIR of its own, and merges the junit files into one; a file that leaves no report or runs over 20 minutes counts as an error case. A file that starts a process pool of its own (test_self_clean) runs alone before the queue. suite.log lists the files that passed first, then the output of every file that did not and a summary with one FAILED line per file. The suite unit gets CPUQuota=400% (the other units keep 200%). The task's own check with test paths and the rerun of A7 keep the one process.
- Drafting skill (Q-0015, edit mode): all fixes of one message go in one pass, the master in the target language is fixed first and the Russian reading copy made again from it, each round shows the whole Russian text from the first line to the last with no change list, a small edit gets only `awb write keep` and `awb write check` while the review runs once at the end, the length stays at the budget set from the request, and a named meeting or German reader gets the German version at once. Tests: `tests/test_drafting_edit_mode.py`.

### Fixed
- `seal/setup.sh` keeps the owner's line `doc_hosts` of `paths.conf` when it rewrites the host file. The deploy of 2026-11-24 dropped it, so `awb doc hosts add` refused until the line was put back by hand.
- `awb service update` in an installed release (read-only rules folder) ended in a PermissionError traceback; it now says in one line to run it in the repository checkout, commit `rules/` and deploy, and writes nothing. The service description of 2026-11-18 is in `rules/services.tsv` (79 services, unchanged list).
- `awb review` l0 no longer blocks a deliverable on "service: X is not offered on TCP" when every sentence that names X says it is no longer offered (discontinued, withdrawn, not available and the like); a sentence that proposes X is still a finding; the chat note keeps counting every mention (tcp-ui5o ask of 2026-11-22).
- `awb side take` copies only a result the side session wrote (tcp-6edq ask 2026-11-21-tcp-6edq-1: a take of three failed side sessions with no commit wrote `notes/side-NAME.md` with an old result, because a merged side session had brought its `SIDE-RESULT.md` into the project and every new worktree started with it): `start` records the start commit, and a `SIDE-RESULT.md` equal to the one of that commit (the merge base of the project and `side/NAME` for an older record) is no result: take writes nothing and says so, `awb side status` shows `result: no`. Tests: `tests/test_6edq_side_take_old_result.py`.
- The stop hook asks no side session for STATE.md and OPEN.md (tcp-6edq asks 2026-11-21-tcp-6edq-2 and 2026-11-21-tcp-6edq-3: in the worktree `.side/NAME` on the branch `side/NAME` it asked to update both after a commit, while the side rules give both files to the main session): `side.is_side_worktree` tells a side session by its folder (a linked worktree `.side/NAME`) or its branch (`side/NAME`), and there the stop hook leaves out the lag reminder of STATE.md and OPEN.md and the OPEN.md sweep; the main session is asked as before. Tests: `tests/test_6edq_side_stop.py`.
- `awb howto render` renders fenced code blocks (tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-22-tcp-bytg-3: a block of three backticks and a language came out as one paragraph with the backticks and the language word, its line breaks lost and a star pair in italics): a block between three or more backticks or tildes becomes `<pre><code class="language-X">` with its lines and indents kept and every character escaped, a block indented under a step stays in that step and the list goes on, a block without its closing fence ends with the draft. Tests: `tests/test_howto_code_blocks.py`.
- A code block stays on one page in `awb pdf` (tcp-bytg ask 2026-11-22-tcp-bytg-2: six blocks of a guide were split over a page break, so the page number line such as `21 / 32` lay inside the copied block): a code block or a text table that fits a page goes on one page and moves to the next one when it does not fit where it starts; a block longer than a page breaks between its lines, never inside a wrapped line, with the page number below it. Tests: `tests/test_pdf_code_pages.py`.
- A console password stays out of a lab run log (tcp-6edq ask 2026-11-22-tcp-6edq-1: the generated ubuntu password an Ubuntu 24.04 image prints on its serial console was written unmasked into a full run log): `awb/labsecrets.py` masks the `user:password` lines after the cloud-init line "Set the following 'random' passwords", the `user:password` pairs of a chpasswd line and its list and `password for USER: x`, also inside the JSON of an os-getConsoleOutput answer where a line break is the escape `\n`; a password or login field right after such an escaped line break is masked too. Both console paths use it: the answer of the key service (`labadmin.shorten_answer`) and the run log and output of `awb.labclient`, so a script reads the console with the password masked as well. Tests: `tests/test_6edq_console_mask.py`.
- Code lines stay whole in `awb pdf` (tcp-bytg ask 1): a code block whose longest line is wider than the page gets a smaller Courier for the whole block, down to 6 pt (`CODE_FLOOR`), until that line fits, so a PowerShell pipe or a quoted registry path copied from the PDF is one line again; a line too long even at 6 pt does not count for the size and wraps as before with the drawn arrow; a block that fits keeps 9 pt. 5 tests in `tests/test_pdf_code_lines.py`.
- A run log that left git is still the run log (tcp-bytg): `awb lab obs put` and `get` were refused once `notes/lab-runs/<run>.log` had moved to `full/` by the 2 MiB rule, because the key service took only the old path. Every reader and writer now finds a run log through one helper (`awb/lablog.py`: `places`, `live_path`, `open_live`): the log at its old place, else its full log. After a move every append goes to the full log; a late file at the old place is merged into it first, so a second move loses no line
- The name check tries a transient answer of the vault daemon again (tcp-bytg): its rate limit (also every scan slot busy), a reload (`busy`) and a connection it closed before it answered (`VaultDropped`, a hand-over or the connection cap of the user) get four more tries 0.2, 0.5, 1 and 2 seconds apart (`check.TRANSIENT_DELAYS`); when it still fails the message says which of them it was. A locked vault, a refused user and no daemon are not tried again. `awb kb` now passes that reason on instead of "the register cannot be read", which stays for a broken register read here (`awb kb find` failed 4 of 15 times in a side session under the other checks of the same user). `awb dev ask` runs in a side session's worktree (`.side/NAME`) and writes the ask of the project whose `.git` the worktree's git link leads into; a `.side` folder that is no such worktree is refused. Tests: `tests/test_bytg_retry_and_side_ask.py`.
- TMS reads work (tcp-ui5o): TMS, a global service, answered every call 401 TMS.0003. A key-signed call to `tms` (`awb cloud call`, the key service, a write key of a customer tenant in a lab run) now carries the account in a signed `X-Domain-Id` header, read once from IAM `/v3/auth/domains`; a lab call with the admin login takes the domain token for `tms` as for `iam`. A lab call that a service answers 401 reaches the session as `HTTP 401` and the service's error code (`HTTP 401 TMS.0003`), never its message, in place of "the key service refused the request". Tests: `tests/test_tms_domain.py`.
- UX10 (his yes 2026-11-12): an assistant session of the owner user runs `awb start query|project --goal ... [--from DIR --trusted]` without `--customer` and `--review-tier`, `awb import CODE --trusted DIR|FILE...` (a folder argument now takes its regular files), `awb import CODE` of the bucket folder (fetched under `bucket.import_door()`, never the vault inbox there), `awb winlab open` and `awb winlab close`. Still refused there: `--customer`, `--review`, `--redo`, `--paste`, a forms prompt, `awb winlab add|remove`, `open --public-image`, `awb tenant mode` and `awb bucket pull`, and now also `awb register add` (a written form is a name); the work user is refused all of them as before. Tests: `tests/test_ux10_assistant.py`.
- `awb winlab get` of a disk image (owner decision 2026-11-20, tcp-bytg): a VHDX, VHD, VMDK, QCOW2, ISO or raw IMG from a lab machine is our own lab output and gets no name check by default, by the rule to check once at entry (customer images enter only through `awb intake`); after the SHA-256 compare it gets the secret check (none in a public image window) and is published, and the run log and the project ledger get one line with file, size, SHA-256 and "own lab output, no name check". Every other file kind keeps its checks. `--check` runs the name check on an image; a plain `--recheck` of a kept image follows the same default (size and SHA-256 verified, same log and ledger line, published) and `--recheck --check` runs the name check; an image over 2 GB is checked over the unique text runs of its bytes (ASCII and UTF-8, UTF-16 in both byte orders, zero blocks and repeats dropped) in paced requests of 400,000 characters with a progress line and a resume mark, instead of one request per part of every 4 MB piece (444 MB of a 21.9 GB image took 70 minutes under the daemon's 16 MB a minute). Tests: `tests/test_winlab_recheck_fast.py`.
- `sudo awb deploy` (Q-0002, second step) restarts every active service of the web side (one a `restart` line of `seal/web/install.sh` names) in the same deploy, even when its code did not change, with the reason `runs the release before this one`, so needrestart is clean right after the deploy. The first step caught the gateway only one deploy late when it had started after the switch before (C-326, f79af81). Only with the web side live; the key service, the oneshot of the owner status, the sockets and the build runner keep their rules.
- `sudo awb deploy` (Q-0002) restarts an active service that runs an older release even when its code did not change: its main process started before the switch of the last deploy and the journal does not record it on the live release. The plan and the dry run show it as `restart` with `runs an older release (started before the switch to COMMIT)`, and it goes through the same restart, wait and result as any other unit. The gateway of the console (socket activated) kept a release the deploy then pruned, needrestart flagged it and the host monitor saw the portal down after most deploys. The build runner keeps its deferral, a timer's service its next run, the vault and the key service their own rules.
- The shared folder `recheck` is created group writable with the setgid bit (2775) and `state.tsv`, `refuted.tsv` and `requested.tsv` in it are written 664, so the owner can append the requests of `awb refresh` after the work side created the folder. A narrower folder or file is widened by the user who owns it; a folder of another user that is too narrow stops the request with one line that names the mode and the chmod its owner runs, and the refresh shows that line as the error of its part (Q-0001: a folder of mode 2755 stopped the weekly refresh of 2026-11-14). Tests: `tests/test_recheck_folder_mode.py`.
- `tests/test_web_owner_api.py`: the request helper of the owner actions test reads a broken pipe on the send as the server closing before a byte was read, which is what a refused peer does when it wins the race under four workers (C-321, C-323); every assert stays as it was.
- `awb queue` checks a row once when it is added or changed and keeps the verdict per row with the register stamp in `queue-verdicts.tsv` (signed with a key in the owner's state folder); list, add, next and done check only rows with no verdict or an old stamp, and a row with a finding still refuses. The vault daemon answers a new `revision` op on the check socket, an opaque mark of the register, the allow list and the daemon, so one request replaces about 260 checks and two calls in a row no longer run out of the 600 per minute (Q-0011, UXR12). A structural test lists the modules allowed to call the name check; a new caller fails it until it is added on purpose. Tests: `tests/test_queue_check_once.py`.
- A test that asserts how long something took carries `@pytest.mark.wallclock` (26 tests in 16 files), and the suite unit of the build check runs those tests alone before the queue of four files at a time, the rest of their file still in the queue; a test pins that every wall-clock bound in `tests/` is marked. The bounds stay as they were (Q-0028: a bound of 30 s took 32.7 s next to three other files, check C-314).
- The ledger draft of the stop hook takes links, bare host names and other structured values out of the commit subjects (they show as `(url)` and so on) instead of being refused for url on every stop, and commits a session wrote down with `awb ledger add --project CODE` after the last draft or after a refusal are not drafted again; the refusal message names that command (tcp-bytg).
- `awb check` reads a dotted four-part number as a version, not an address, when a version word stands before it in its line (version, release, build, engine, v and others), when the header of its table column is a version word or when its column holds only versions with at least one that is no address; a real address in prose and a network with a prefix stay addresses. The Write hook's secret masker leaves a plain word in a Markdown heading (`# Password: policy and rotation`, `## Bearer authentication`); a value with a digit, a symbol or quotes stays masked (tcp-6ntg). Tests: `tests/test_check_versions.py`.
- `awb winlab get` no longer deletes a received file when the name check cannot run: the file stays unpublished as `FILE.awb-part` with its SHA-256 and the progress of the checks, and the same get with the new `--recheck` checks it again from there without a transfer or an open window. A disk image over 2 GB gets the name check per piece of its bytes instead of the whole check, which reads the file into memory (tcp-bytg: a 21.9 GB VHDX was deleted after 1 h 45 of transfer). A name or a secret still deletes the file. Tests: `tests/test_winlab_get_large_image.py`.
- A delete of a resource listed in RESOURCES.md with `delete_listed=True` that answers 2xx or 404 is recorded in the run as removed, `awb lab run end` counts and lists it, labclient sets its RESOURCES.md row to deleted and a kept create with that id leaves `awb owner status` (tcp-6edq: three listed deletes were reported as removed 0).
- The lab run log keeps a boot script (`OS-EXT-SRV-ATTR:user_data`, `user_data`, the CCE pre and post install scripts) only as a placeholder with its length, for every server, base64 or not: `GET .../cloudservers/detail` answers the user_data of other projects' servers (tcp-s5i3). The script still gets the whole answer.
- The commit gate checks a lab run log for secrets, private keys and tokens only, no longer for home paths: a home path is no secret and our own output is checked once at entry (tcp-s5i3, tcp-bytg).
- `awb side stop`: a client that was reaped between the check by signal and the read of its `/proc` entry no longer counts as alive (the cause of the flaky test_stop_kills_a_client_that_ignores_termination on a loaded build host, Q-0013).
- The secret wipe of a put and of `awb clean` no longer reads the value after `-p` as a password when the command takes `-p` as parents, port, pid or preserve (`mkdir -p /var/tmp/tcp`, `install -d`, `ssh -p 22`, `docker run -p 8080:80`, `psql -p 5432`) or when the value is a path (tcp-bytg: a guide command reached the customer broken in two puts). The `-p` of `mysql` and `sshpass` stays a secret in every form, and the glued quoted form `-p'VALUE'` is now caught too. Tests: `tests/test_put_p_flag.py`.
- The secret masker of the Write hook, the put scrub and the commit gate no longer read a PowerShell call or variable as a password: a prompt for a password with Read-Host, Get-Credential, an env variable, a cmdlet in parentheses or ConvertTo-SecureString of a variable is no secret. The call became [secret] and the script broke (tcp-renz). A literal stays a secret, and a literal after a PowerShell password parameter or after ConvertTo-SecureString is now caught too, where it was left visible before. Tests: `tests/test_put_p_flag.py`.
- Refresh hardening: validate result and calibration fields, refuse linked, special and oversized inputs, pin file access, serialize runs and compound applies, publish private files atomically, screen secrets in text and JSON (our own output, so no name check), and withhold dependency values. Treat unreadable project work and damaged wipe history as failures. Add 87 fault-checked regression cases in `tests/test_refresh.py`, including existing read refusals and ordinary inputs outside the project.
- The commit gate scans the pieces of a text over 4 Mi characters in up to four forked worker processes, with every check as before: a 105 MB lab log took more than 25 minutes on one core (tcp-bytg). A process with other threads or one that cannot fork scans in itself.
- The commit gate gives the log of a lab run (`notes/lab-runs/*.log`, written by awb/labclient.py) the secret check only, as `awb paste --own` does: it is our own output, checked once at entry, and the name check of the vault daemon made a 105 MB log of tcp-bytg take more than 20 minutes. That log now takes about 8 minutes on four cores.
- The build check gives the whole suite 45 minutes instead of 30 (stopgap of Q-0013): it took 26 to 29 minutes and every check hung while several build sessions ran at once.
- `awb winlab get` checks the received file in place under a private name instead of a second copy, so a large image needs its own size on disk once, not twice (a 21.9 GB image of tcp-bytg ended in OSError on a full disk). A failure that is no refusal gives the part back, so the same command resumes it.
- Knowledge base hardening: bound file and standard-input reads, refuse linked and special storage, pin directory access, preserve concurrent exports and entry collisions, and roll back entry changes when index publication fails. Screen indexes, benchmark misses and entry metadata, withhold private paths and dependency diagnostics, and fail closed on damaged scope files or unavailable name checks. Add 35 fault-checked regressions in `tests/test_kb.py`, including existing writer serialization and missing-file guarantees.
- `awb check` and every other structured check no longer read a rate of the live price API as a phone number: a number with a dot and 4 to 6 decimals is a price next to a currency, "rate", "hour" or "/h", under a price column or in a table column that holds only prices; an ISO date (2026-11-19) or a German date (19.11.2026) is never a phone number nor a part of one, also when two stand in adjacent table cells. Real numbers next to them are still found (tcp-renz).
- `labclient keep` keeps the mode of RESOURCES.md when it adds a row (a new file gets 664), so the lab daemon still reads it and the next keep is not refused with "keep needs a live row" (tcp-6edq).
- The token of an ECS VNC console link (`.../novnc/vnc_auto.html?token=UUID`) stays whole in the lab shortener, the commit gate and the `awb winlab put` check, so a console link reaches the owner usable (tcp-bytg). The commit gate scans a text over 4 Mi characters in pieces of 1 Mi with 256 lines of overlap: a 63 MB lab log no longer gets it killed for memory.
- The pre-tool hook no longer refuses a call that cds into its project as "the project folder of this session cannot be determined" when the call carries a long quoted text (tcp-x7k2: every `awb kb add` with a long statement). A part of a path over 255 bytes names no file, so the guard reads it as a missing place as written instead of taking the kernel's ENAMETOOLONG for an unknown project; such a path under another project still refuses, and so does a call outside every project. Tests: `tests/test_pretool_folder.py`.
- PDF writer hardening: pin and bound file reads, refuse linked and escaping paths and special files, publish complete private PDFs without changing the process umask, screen names and secrets in text, metadata, links and paths, and withhold diagnostic values. Bound hostile parsing and layout, preserve unfinished HTML content and titles, and count pages independently of document text. Add 52 fault-checked regression tests in `tests/test_pdf.py`.
- Dataset build hardening: pin and bound inputs, validate snapshot and service data, fail closed on damaged recheck records, stage and serialize builds with replacement rollback, screen generated text for names and secrets, withhold diagnostic values, quote download verification arguments and repair live price checks. Added 37 regression tests, including fault checks of existing tag and secret screening.
- Intake hardening: validate inputs and redo archives, refuse links and special files, extract private snapshots, recheck changed sources, preserve archive collisions and earlier outputs on rollback, scrub reader notes and public reports, and withhold dependency error values. Add 62 fault-checked regression tests in `tests/test_intake.py`, including text callbacks, concurrent imports and fresh-export coverage.
- Office extractor hardening: pin regular input files and refuse links and special files; bound XML, table expansion and child reads; keep hidden cells out of visible text; retain detection values and mark missing, malformed or skipped parts incomplete; contain read and write errors without values. Add 45 fault-checked regression tests, including concurrent child isolation, private temporary files, traversal refusal and ordinary sources outside the project.
- Price book hardening: bound and validate specification and workbook inputs, refuse linked and special files and escaping project paths, screen names, secrets and diagnostic values, serialize builds, publish private output pairs with rollback, and validate and lock calculation records. Compare pooled hours and start dates without prices. Preserve CRLF specifications. Add 70 fault-checked regressions in `tests/test_pricebook.py`, including decoded JSON privacy, arithmetic refusals, calculation record integrity and identifier exhaustion, and failed publication cleanup.
- `awb winlab get` of a compressed file or a disk image is checked for names whole again (the check opens the archive) at any size and with the fast secret check per piece; the name check per 4 MiB piece of its bytes took hours on the rate limit and missed a name inside a compressed file over 64 MB.
- Lab object storage hardening: pin and bound record and vendor-list reads, validate identifiers and metadata, refuse unsafe download descriptors and escaping project paths, serialize job admission and log blocks, recheck closing runs and write access, clean up failed starts, preserve colliding job records, restart unsafe resumes, check download size and response headers, and screen names, secrets and dependency errors. Added 43 regression tests in `tests/test_lo_lab_obs.py`, including a fault check for existing missing-file and invalid-JSON handling.
- Tool guard hardening: reject malformed calls, hidden shell substitutions, unsafe project and workflow metadata, and unreadable path resolution; bound parsing and file reads; pin and serialize audit access, preserve complete rows, and withhold private identifiers and diagnostic values. Added 26 regression tests, including existing invalid-text refusals and fresh-export coverage.
- Deliverable review hardening: refuse linked, special, oversized and escaping inputs; pin atomic writes and serialize review mutations; validate contract, claim, lens and record fields; withhold names, secrets and dependency error values; repair non-text snapshots and remove old records after failed passes. Lens binding names are hashed, so earlier records with lenses need a new pass. Added 47 regression tests, preserving evidence directories and existing send-gate behavior.
- Documentation fetch hardening: reject encoded traversal and malformed replies, bound host files and PDF process output, refuse linked and special storage, serialize host additions, publish complete private output files and withhold names, secrets and unchecked errors. Add 46 fault-checked regression tests in `tests/test_doc.py`.
- Bucket client hardening: validate and bound service replies and pull history, refuse linked and special files, pin file access, publish complete private downloads, serialize local transfers, review and scrub one upload snapshot, recheck moved objects before deletion and keep private names and values out of metadata and errors. Add 54 fault-checked regressions in `tests/test_bucket.py`, including write failures, directory swaps, publication races and metadata checks; reject repeated trailing slashes in keys and NUL bytes in `.markdown` files. Preserve uploads from ordinary files outside the project.
- Egress filter hardening: validate allow rows, DNS replies, cached addresses, counters and public status; refuse linked, special, oversized and escaping file access; serialize mode changes and loads; restore configuration after failed switches; withhold unchecked command diagnostics and parser values. Added 26 regression tests, including atomic publication and missing-file guarantees.
- Windows lab review: validate complete identifiers, registry and job metadata and broker replies; refuse linked, special and oversized storage; serialize registry changes and project operations; keep dependency values and unchecked paths out of output; transfer checked private copies, pin download directories and publish without overwriting concurrent files. Check large downloads and secrets, and withhold oversized output and split log lines. Added 74 regression cases in `tests/test_wl_registry.py` and `tests/test_wl_session.py`, including existing read and write failure guarantees.
- Vault hardening: refuse linked and special storage, pin file access, preserve changed plaintext and conflicting ciphertext, serialize sealing and log headers, validate every encryption retry file, keep unavailable or changed encrypted registers locked, invalidate stale caches, and withhold parser error values. Bound pipelined connection lifetimes and clean up failed starts. Added 40 regression tests in `tests/test_vault.py`, each failing against the previous code.
- Lab client hardening: validate identifiers and service replies, pin file access, refuse links and special files, serialize resource and run updates, publish markers atomically, preserve newer downloads and run markers, and withhold names, secrets and unchecked error values. Added 35 regression tests in `tests/test_lr_lab_runs.py`, including export portability and fault checks. The key service's own one-line refusals still reach the session and the run log when the screen leaves them unchanged, an obs transfer logs its file and object again and a withheld log block keeps its blank separator line.
- Project administration refuses linked and special register, lock and goal files, withholds secrets in edited goals, rejects invisible register fields, and reports unreadable checks without values. Delete requires a readable resource table, checks missing folders and held sessions, and anchors removal to checked directories. Regression tests cover failures, concurrent deletes, list privacy and sync selection in a fresh export.
- Windows lab review fix: lab machine names and the broker's window refusal are generated text and get the pattern and secret check only, no name check (`check_own`), so a session list and a run no longer fail when the name check is unavailable. The lock of the registry and of a project's lab work waits at most 60 seconds, then the command is refused, so a held lock never blocks a command or a test run without end; the window refusal pattern comes from `awb.codes`.

### Added
- UX21: the owner page lists checked project Decisions from the timer status file through `GET /api/owner/items`. Take the default sends the UX20 call once, stays disabled while pending and after success, and shows sent to the session; refusals show their reason. Items without a default show the UX18 app line. Original OPEN.md indexes survive filtering. Owner-actions retains status-file-only access. Tests: `tests/test_owner_page_decisions.py`.

Owner hook settings support writing before compaction, showing the file time at session start and one reminder after the second compaction. Host installation remains a development action on the owner's word. Tests: `tests/test_handover_write.py`.
- UX20 follow-up: route take-default to `/run/awb-project-create.sock`, where the create service in the `projects` role runs as `awb` and checks the owner level header before reading and writing project data. Restore owner actions to its previous scope without widening `awb-owner` access. Tests cover project socket routing and the create service role, peer and level checks.
- UX20: `POST /api/owner/items/take-default {code, item}` on the owner host in development writes a fixed default note through the project note writer, its prompt notice and one ledger line. The service reads the indexed owner item itself; owner sign-in, origin and CSRF checks match publish. Inactive projects and invalid indexes are refused, extra body text is ignored and durable receipts suppress repeated or concurrent clicks. The session acts and closes the item under UX6. Tests: `tests/test_owner_take_default.py`.
- UX12: `awb projects note CODE FILE --after COMMIT` holds a note until a successful live release contains its commit. Project prompts show it once per session; deploy ends with a project code and handle for each newly released note, and `awb owner status --dev` lists held notes with their commits. Tests: `tests/test_note_after.py`.

The owner repository prompt shows each ask once per session, `awb owner status --dev` lists open asks and the default status reminds development after 24 hours. `awb projects note CODE --answers FILE --title TEXT NOTE_FILE` delivers a checked answer and closes the ask. Work rules send tooling bugs through `awb dev ask`. Tests: `tests/test_dev_ask.py`.
- UX9: `awb owner status` finds `export-*` folders in the owner's home with a file younger than 30 days and no file in the import history by hash. Tests: `tests/test_ownerwait_exports.py`.

The gate rejects new undated owner items while preserving legacy ones. The stop hook requests the sweep above 20 items once a day per project, and work rules require closure after carrying out a default. Tests: `tests/test_open_sweep.py`.
- SS: `awb side start NAME --brief FILE [--model MODEL] [--effort LEVEL]` lets a project session hand side work (a report, research, an analysis) to a headless side session in the worktree `.side/NAME` with its own brief, its own model and effort and the same rules and hooks; `awb side status`, `take [--merge]`, `stop` and `drop`. The work rules of every session gain the line on when and how to split work off.
