- UX1 regression fix: `awb start` prints preflight before its final navigation lines, preserving the last `app: SSH host ..., folder ...` line with `--from DIR`. The preflight integration test now exercises the start flow.
- UX1: `awb projects preflight CODE` reports granted kind, GET-only tenant access, free EIP/VPC/route-table quotas, host-list membership and lab admin store freshness with codes and handles. Missing or unknown evidence returns 1. Spawn, start and unpark print the report; session-start shows non-ok lines once per session. Inaccessible or malformed owner metadata and login-backed reads stay unknown, with no login attempted. Input symlinks are not followed and help needs no project access. Tests: `tests/test_preflight.py`.
- UX2: `awb owner status` shows decisions only: the creates kept by lab runs are one line per project, a build task stopped by the owner or ended with success is no item, the items that start with `owner (tooling)`, `(tooling)` or `Steering:` leave the list and `--dev` prints them, at most five owner items show per project (oldest first) with the line `and N more: awb owner status --all CODE`, and `--all [CODE]` prints everything as before. The 08:00 mail follows the same rules.
- UX18: the board and project detail APIs add checked waiting items with locations using the owner status command recognizer. Project app destinations use the work host alias and project root in `paths.conf`; restricted logins receive no destination for forbidden projects. The API contract and acceptance tests cover both fields.
- UX5: `awb queue add|next|done|list` keeps open work in `queue.tsv`, archives completed rows in `queue-done.tsv`, marks unchanged next steps stale after 24 hours and shows owner rows with next steps in `awb owner status`. `awb queue migrate` imports open legacy rows once and marks `QUEUE.md` as the archive. The documented keep-the-queue rule names the commands. Tests: `tests/test_work_queue.py`.

Optional owner user units run weekly on Sunday 03:00 UTC; installation is manual.
- UX14: `awb tenant password ALIAS` asks for a changed lab admin password hidden, writes the store entry and loads

Loopback only, refused with one line before anything is sent, never through a proxy; a model that fails costs the piece and the report counts it, with one line of counts per file. `awb clean teach keep|wipe|list` keeps the owner's corrections in `~/awb-laptop/vault/teach.tsv` (600), read by every later run and sent nowhere. `awb clean measure [--llm [URL]]` runs the wipe pack with the rules alone and with the model, leaks and losses per case module. The new case module `calibration/redteam/wipe/cases_meetings.py` holds 40 invented meeting transcripts in English and German; the rules alone leave 17 leaking and 1 losing (EXPECTED.md). No model is trained or fine-tuned.
- B-072: the runner's rebase onto a moved main merges `CHANGELOG.md` and `build/tests.json` with the merge drivers of `awb pr take` (a copy of `awb/mergedriver.py` outside the branch), so two tasks that each add a line to one week no longer end `red: rebase conflict`. A conflict in any other file still does, and the check line now names the files.
- UX11: `Status:` and `Next:` of STATE.md stay at most 300 characters each and say no sentence twice (`status.shape`). The stop hook holds a reply whose STATE.md breaks it, names the two counts and says to move the detail below the two lines. `awb projects sync` reports the projects that break it and changes nothing. The board cuts a longer line at 300 characters with `...`.

A core module that imports the platform package fails it in seconds.

  reads a second list at each fetch, the file `doc_hosts` of `paths.conf` names (the owner writes it, the work user

  the host and `awb owner status` counts them, to commit in one batch.
- CX: `awb codex usage [--json]` reads the Codex quota from `codex app-server` (5-hour and weekly window, plan, reset credits) without spending tokens and without showing account data. `awb codex run TASKFILE [--base REF]` hands a bounded code task (`Paths:` and `Acceptance:` lines, checked as a UI task is) to `codex exec` in a git worktree on `codex/ID`, writes `result.md` with the diff stat and the files changed outside `Paths:` as a refusal, and never commits, pushes or merges; one run at a time. `awb codex status` lists the runs.
- UX3: `awb owner status` shows a command under an item only when it stands in backticks after run, type or his terminal in the same item; a sentence that starts with a command name or a backticked command that is only mentioned gives none, and the place stays project session. The work rules get the line "A command for him in OPEN.md stands in backticks after `run:`" (`awb projects sync` adds it).

The import ends with the gate's check on its copies and says `N copies, gate clean` or the classes left. A file with neither is copied as before.

Redirects only to a listed host, a portal host, a 401/403 or a web page is refused with the hint to hand the download to the owner, at most 16 GB, a missing target folder is named in the refusal, a broken download goes on with a Range request, the vendor hash is checked or the computed SHA-256 and SHA-1 are recorded, and the run log gets URL, file, size and hashes. The work user's egress filter is unchanged. First entries: cdn.watchguard.com, with software.watchguard.com as a portal.
- RB: a read check block can name `region` (the calls go to that region of the tenant) and `expect` (4xx statuses that count as an answer, so a missing endpoint is proven by its 404); a test path may hold spaces inside a selector; `kb how` no longer reads a path such as `subnets.0.id` in the calls or test lines as a web address.

### Changed
- rules/allow-domains.txt: www.nvidia.com, resources.nvidia.com, en.wikipedia.org and de.wikipedia.org, so `awb doc fetch` reads GPU data sheets and encyclopedia pages (the host match is exact).
- rules/allow-domains.txt: watchguard.com and www.watchguard.com, the vendor docs of Firebox V on KVM.

### Fixed
- Rules sync hardening: validate notice codes and field types, bound JSON and rule reads, refuse links and special files, confine registered projects, serialize syncs and session updates, publish private marks and complete notices atomically, and suppress storage values in errors. Added 30 fault-checked regression tests in `tests/test_rulesync.py`.
- Merge driver hardening: refuse ambiguous JSON, invalid dates and Unicode, oversized inputs, unsafe paths and changed snapshots; keep names and values out of diagnostics while retaining short test codes. Preserve metadata deletions and changelog formatting, refuse delete/change conflicts, make large merges linear and publish complete results under an output lock. Add 32 regression tests for parsing, privacy, file failures, path swaps and concurrent runs in `tests/test_build_rebase_drivers.py`.
- Exchange inbox and put hardening: validate names, ids, service errors and hidden file kinds; refuse linked or outside-project inputs, protect intake copies and metadata files from overwrites, serialize takes, check and scrub one upload snapshot, fail closed on unreadable picture checks and hide private filenames, image reasons and exception values. Added 24 regression tests in `tests/test_xchg.py`.
- Harden `awb clean` rule updates against malformed trees, unsafe files, insecure redirects and concurrent staging collisions; reject linked uploads, remove secrets from filenames and diagnostics, and handle output creation races. Add isolated regression tests in `tests/test_clean.py`.
- Sweep hardening (`awb/tcp/sweep.py`): the handle file is read without following a link or waiting on a pipe and refused when oversized or malformed; it is saved through a new private temp file under a lock with a merge of what another run wrote, and a handle two runs gave to two resources stops the second save; mask replaces in one pass; RESOURCES.md links and pipes add no id; lab names and handles take no trailing newline; the report shows only tag values, states and dates of the expected shape; a relative XDG_STATE_HOME is ignored. Tests: `tests/test_sweep_review.py`.
- Send check (`review.send_check`): it fails closed and never raises. An unreadable own project folder, a fifo or device as the file or among the lenses, a linked reviews folder, a malformed project register, an unlistable projects root or an unreadable `hcs-` folder there refuse; every check reads a private copy of the bytes that were hashed and a reviewed deliverable must carry the record's hash, so a file that changes during the check cannot pass; a secret refuses in every file; a request lens with a deliverable field that is no string does not count; a refusal names a project code only, never a folder name. Regression tests: `tests/test_send_check_review.py`.
- Ledger hardening: refuse linked, special and oversized storage, pin directory access, lock reads and roll back failed appends; reject malformed JSON and unsafe Unicode, preserve ambiguous batch findings, bound name-check requests and hide dependency values. Recheck stored text and tags for names and secrets, including encoded forms. Added 48 regression cases with fault checks in `tests/test_ledger.py`.
- Receipt hardening: reject unsafe hook settings, Git overrides and linked worktree registrations; bound key-service replies and mask errors; withhold private session identifiers, serialize receipt starts, anchor storage to checked directories, publish marks atomically and count only complete valid refusal rows. Regression tests: `tests/test_receipt.py`.
- Owner wait and owner status: refuse linked, special, oversized and out-of-project inputs; preserve unknown import history, validate cached decisions, isolate concurrent weekly writes, screen full source text and aggregated fields, and keep private values out of JSON, mail and error output. Regression tests: `tests/test_ownerwait_decisions.py`.
- Deploy review: `awb deploy` validates ids and unit names without letting a trailing newline through, refuses a `--to` ref that git would read as an option or a range, drops status probes that climb out of /run, never writes the journal or the log line through a link, refuses a linked or special lock file and a linked releases folder, replaces a link at the release folder and a leftover folder at `src.next`, refuses an extracted link that resolves out of the release and a corrupt archive as a failed step, fails a step that hits an OSError with exit 1 and its errno text only and relays the plan child's error without control characters. Tests: `tests/test_deploy_review.py`.
- Key service hardening (`awb/tcp/keys.py`): every name, path and project pattern ends with `\Z`, so a service "iam" with a trailing newline no longer passes the IAM guard of write mode; a `.` path segment is refused, so it no longer fills the `{id}` of a lab_writes triple; the call log keeps the project only as a project code; the admin socket has its own connection count, so sessions holding the call socket full cannot lock the owner out; the whole request line has the read timeout; descriptors beyond the limit are closed as they arrive; a request of an unforeseen shape gets one refusal that names the fault kind and no value. Tests: `tests/test_keys_review.py`.
- Hooks hardening: the prompt hook finds pasted blocks and the stop hook finds draft paths in linear time (both patterns hung past the budget, where the client lets the input through); the budget now covers the reading of the hook input and is no longer swallowed by a part that steps back; an internal error of the prompt, write and tool hooks and write hook input that is no JSON object block; pre-write checks every path field and each reading of a path with a variable, a ~ or a `..` after a link; post-write checks the file the tool wrote, refuses a path that reads as two files and finds secrets in a text file with a byte that is no UTF-8; the draft gate reads transcript entries of another shape; the start skips a named pipe and a project file linked from outside the project. Tests: `tests/test_hooks_review.py`.
- Tenants hardening: `awb tenant` refuses an alias, domain id, region, topic, date or project code with a trailing newline, a key reference with a tab or line character (a forged register row), a link in place of the register, the events file or a snapshot's temp file and a FIFO as a key file; two adds or two snapshots at once take a lock and write each row and event once; an error never echoes an unknown alias, a key in a request path or an answer's error text, a domain name from the token loses its control characters and a broken snapshot or register ends with exit 1. Tests: `tests/test_tenants_review.py`.
- Key exchange review: a session GET that grew past max_mb after the head is refused, a failed GET leaves no temporary file and every temporary file's descriptor is closed (GET and the lab take leaked one per call); INDEX.md that grew past its limit or names a length that is no number reads as none; the HTML page of a put takes no title from a --name the name check finds; object keys with control characters are refused; one failing row of an archive never stops the others or the INDEX.md rewrite and its line names the error type only. Tests: `tests/test_keys_exchange_review.py`.
- Gate review: the commit gate finds a key, a token, a home path, an id or a name written with Cyrillic, Greek or fullwidth lookalike letters, a name in a UTF-8 file with one stray byte, a key in a comment line git keeps (`git commit -m`), the lower case and Git Bash spellings of a Windows home folder, the target of a link (also a link to a folder) and text a PDF carries outside its pages; a named pipe or another special file is opaque and never hangs `awb gate DIR`; a refusal prints `[withheld]` for a path part that holds a token or a key. Long runs of slashes, dashes, dots, assignments, attribute pairs, key ids or defanged host labels no longer cost n squared steps in the gate and in the structured patterns (`awb/patterns.py`). Tests: `tests/test_gate_review.py`.
- UX4 hardening: `awb dev ask` rejects malformed metadata and unsafe Unicode, masks hostile parser errors, anchors request and receipt access to opened directories, publishes complete requests atomically, and serializes answers using one checked snapshot. Added regression tests for privacy, failed writes, external paths and concurrent runs.
- UX1 hardening: preflight pins directory descriptors, refuses linked and nonregular evidence, bounds reads and traversal, rejects ambiguous grants and malformed callbacks, and withholds unchecked output fields. Missing evidence stays unknown; IPv6 links are checked. Regression tests cover path swaps, read failures and concurrent read-only runs.
- UX5: harden queue storage against links, special files and reserved filenames; reject malformed TSV, inconsistent completion records and duplicate migration columns; keep parser and backend errors free of input values and sync archive renames before removing open work. Safety verdicts now stay in process memory, keyed by register contents, with no cache reuse for an unknown register revision. Add 27 regression tests covering these defects, concurrent writers, interrupted writes and migration retries.
- UX6 hardening: open sweep and close refuse linked, special and oversized files, isolate Git receipts to the project, preserve significant receipt punctuation, suppress hostile argument and dependency values, match Markdown fences correctly, tick compact checkboxes, withhold terminal controls and reject overlapping closes with changed numbering. Readers share the close lock and reminder claims stay private and serialized. Regression tests cover each fix and existing failure, receipt and metadata guarantees.
- LX-5 hardening: exit packs anchor file operations to opened directories, refuse links and unexpected file types, preserve the previous pack on publication failure, isolate and validate Git metadata, validate work archives, and keep argument values and private paths out of diagnostics and manifests. Regression tests cover missing and unreadable inputs, concurrent runs, path swaps and secret-free summaries.
- UX21 hotfix: `awb owner status --write` rebuilds the decisions of the owner page at most every 15 minutes and keeps the last list in between; a rebuild each minute used up the owner's name check budget. A read of `queue.tsv` sends only new or edited rows to the name check (a cache by row content and register). The batch name check halves a chunk with a finding instead of checking each of its texts alone. The hand-over writes the home in the commands of owner status as ~ (its secret check refused the export commands of B-060), and the subjects of its git log get the secret check only
- UX13 hardening: anchor hand-over and compaction writes to opened directories, refuse linked or special queue and output files, bound reads, isolate Git from inherited repository settings, hide hostile argument values, reject malformed receipts and retain late compaction reminders. Regression tests cover failure preservation, privacy and concurrent writes.
- SS: harden side sessions against linked paths, malformed or unreadable files, unsafe output, stale process IDs, overlapping commands and partial Git or client failures; validate worktree gate registration and preserve empty commits on merge. Add regression tests in `tests/test_side.py`.
- UX17: three false findings of the name check. The code view now covers `.ps1` and `.psm1`, and a token right after `$` or a dot is no bank code and no web address there. A number with a decimal separator and a currency or a price column near it (`0.123456 EUR`) is no phone number. `awb review init` accepts the `<file>.de.md`, `.en.md` and `.ru.md` names that `awb write translate` writes (the stem is checked as before).
- IST: `awb import` failed with PermissionError after the originals had moved when an older intake-report.md of
  another user sat in the sticky outbox folder. The intake now refuses before anything is written or moved and names
  the rename that clears it; a report that still fails to write takes the run back.
- tests/test_self_clean.py scans the files in one process per core: one process took 140 s of the 120 s limit, in the
  secret, token, blocklist and home path patterns over 13 MB. The scan and what it checks are the same.

## Week 45, 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-08
### Added

- `awb write check` blocks a line on what was not tested, verified or proven in mode mail and doc (English, German and Russian forms, class weakness), and the project rules and the drafting skill say it: a text for others says what to do and what holds, the state of an internal run stays in STATE.md and the chat. Tests: `tests/test_voice_tells.py`.

- `awb owner status --mail` cuts a list longer than the 4000 characters the key service's notify takes at whole items and ends it with one line that counts what was left out; a longer list was refused and no morning mail went out since at least 2026-11-06. Tests: `tests/test_nightly_limits.py`.

- `awb check learn` asks the name check at most 500 times a minute (`CHECKS_A_MINUTE`) and waits for the next minute after that: one check per candidate ran into the 600 a minute of the vault daemon and the nightly run failed with CheckRateLimited. Tests: `tests/test_nightly_limits.py`.

- `awb doc fetch` reads a gzip body: the docs host of TCP sends gzip since 2026-11 although the reader asks for identity, so every page there was refused. The body is unpacked under the same cap (5 MB, 32 MB for a PDF); a broken gzip and every other encoding are still refused. Tests: `tests/test_doc.py`.

- `awb tenant cost` prices an EVS disk by the price record whose flavor is `vss.<type>` first: the type SSD (ultra-high I/O) matched the three records of SSD, GP SSD and Extreme SSD by the word and stayed without a price (KB-GL4O). Tests: `tests/test_tenant_cost.py`.

- `awb tenant setup ALIAS` without `--domain-id` takes the domain of the admin token and names it with the first 6 characters of its id; when pass holds a read key that awb-read-<number> does not own (a tenant connected before T10), setup finds the user of that key with the admin token (GET /v3.0/OS-CREDENTIAL/credentials/{key}, the key never printed) and puts it into awb-read (created with Tenant Guest inherited on all projects when missing) instead of a new user and key, then takes a snapshot and runs the cost estimate. It deletes no key, user or group, refuses a key of the admin user and a second run says nothing to do (B-050, branch task/B-050). README and docs/commands-guide.md describe it. Tests: `tests/test_tenant_setup_adopt.py`.

- awb/inventory.py, filesinv.py, filesplan.py and filestidy.py are no longer command only: since BK4 awb/bucket.py archives through filestidy and the key service runs it, so a change of one restarts the units that reach awb/bucket.py.

- The archive rule where it arises (BK4, B-049, branch task/B-049; D-B1). `awb fetch CODE` archives after the download every put whose INDEX.md row says fetched 30 days or more before today (`ARCHIVE_DAYS`): it moves to `<code>/_archive/<YYYY-MM of the put>/from-session/...` of the lab bucket with the HTML page of a Markdown put, the key service copies, compares size and md5 and then deletes the source (the new op `obs` ARCHIVE, the owner's processes only, refused to any other uid like FETCHED) and INDEX.md drops the row of a moved put. One line `archived N put(s) fetched 30 days or more ago` counts a failed move too, which leaves the source and its row and never stops the fetch; `--no-archive` skips it. `awb bucket put CODE FILE` moves an older version of the same group in `out/` (project, normalised stem, suffix, the rule of awb/inventory.py) to `<YYYY-MM>/<code>/_archive/<stamp>/<name>` first (the new admin op `bucket_archive`, the same copy, compare and delete) and then puts the new file; a file with the size and md5 of one of its group there is not put again (one line says so); `--replace` keeps its meaning for the same name. The copy, compare and delete of `awb files tidy` is `filestidy.move_object` now, shared by the three. README ("The archive without a plan"), docs/commands-guide.md and COMMANDS.md describe it. Tests: `tests/test_bk4_archive.py`.

- The PDF cap of `awb doc fetch` is 32 MB, its own value: 25 MB spelled the console upload limit, which only `awb/bucket_layout.py` may write (test_bucket_layout).

- `awb tenant cost` prices an elastic IP with the record whose flavor is eip (OTC_ELASTIC_IP); eu-de also lists "Elastic IP - Address" at 0, and the two made every EIP "no price" on the first live run.

- The monthly cost estimate of the test tenants (B-048, branch task/B-048). New `awb tenant cost [--json]` (owner side, `awb/tcp/tenantcost.py`, loaded by name) prices the latest snapshot of each tenant with one fetch of the live price API per region, PAYG and 730 h a month: a running server by the record of its flavor plus OS (Windows, else Open Linux), a stopped one at 0 with its disks still counted (KB-VTTM, a stopped GPU flavor notes KB-NAAF), a disk per GB and month, an elastic IP x 730 without bandwidth. An item without a price record is listed as no price and the total says how many it leaves out. Sums per tenant, kind and project (a disk without the awb-project tag takes its server's), the top items and the waste groups (disks of stopped servers, disks not attached, elastic IPs not bound); the tenants whose read key could not list a kind are named as unknown. It writes `tenants/cost.json` of the shared folder (aliases, handles, codes, the fetch time, the age of the API's cache, the notes) and GET /api/tenants carries it as `cost` (contract and openapi.yaml). `awb tenant cost note HANDLE TEXT` keeps an owner note (`tenants/cost-notes.tsv`) that takes the item out of every waste group. The snapshot keeps `os` of a server and `attached` of a disk (older snapshots still price) and `awb tenant snapshot` runs the estimate after it. Tests: `tests/test_tenant_cost.py`

- `awb doc fetch` reads a PDF (B-047, done in the steering session while the runner had no API credit): `application/pdf` or an octet stream starting with `%PDF-`, up to 25 MB, through `pdftotext -layout` on a private temporary file with a 60 s timeout, a line `--- page N ---` before each page and `, pdf, N pages` in the source line; encrypted, broken and scanned PDFs are refused with the reason. Checked live on the TCP service description (115 pages). The two PDF cases of `test_a_type_that_is_not_text_is_refused` left that test by the owner's order of 2026-11-07; tests/test_doc_pdf.py holds the new rule.

- The sign-in page of the console in the pult style: dark with the editor orange by default, the light theme in magenta when the browser kept that choice, Inter from the gateway.

- The suite reads the same in an assistant session as in the build runner: a test no longer sees the session markers (CLAUDECODE, CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT) of the shell it was started from and runs with the runner's umask 022. The four tests that were always red in a session (two of awb bucket, two of the dataset put) are green there now.

- The console page ships with `sudo awb deploy` and is held to the API contract (B-046, branch task/B-046). After the units the deploy publishes `awb/tcp/web/portal.html` of the release when its bytes differ from the live page, through the checks (as the owner) and the backup of `awb web publish FILE` (`publish.release_page`, backup `index.html.before-deploy-<commit>`) and prints one line: `page published (backup NAME)`, `page unchanged`, `page refused (REASON, the live page stays)` or, on a host without the web folder, `page skipped`. A refused page never fails the deploy; `--dry-run` prints what the step would do; a change of the page restarts no unit (`PACKAGE_DATA`). `awb web publish FILE` and `--rollback` stay for the owner. New `tests/test_portal_page.py`: every API path the page calls is in `docs/api/openapi.yaml`, every top-level field of `/api/board` is read by the page or listed with a reason (`platform`, `parked` and `build` for now), the page passes the checks of publish.py and the deploy step publishes, leaves, refuses and skips. README ("The board and the console", "Deploy") and INTERFACES.md describe it.

- The console page uses Inter (rsms/inter 4.1, InterVariable.woff2, SIL OFL 1.1, the licence in `awb/tcp/web/fonts/`): the gateway serves `/fonts/InterVariable.woff2` itself from `/srv/awb-web/fonts/`, only when the file has the SHA-256 pinned in the gateway (a binary never enters the repository), before the login, with a long cache, since the page's CSP allows a font only from its own origin; system fonts stay as the fallback.

- Every screen of the console in the pult style: the project page has one status (no third copy), a header with the goal, the code and the state, four numbers, the tabs in one card and the chat beside it (sticky); panels, buttons, tables, tabs, menu and top bar share one denser set of rules in both themes.

- The console page in the "pult" design (owner's choice of 2026-11-07): dark by default with the orange and blue of his code editor (#f0b167 accent, #82b6fc codes), light keeps the Telekom magenta, a switch in the top bar remembered in the browser; every colour is a theme token; the overview is board first (status strip with the TCP platform, the build line and the tenants, five numbers, Waiting on you, the active projects, the platform events, the build queue); the Board page shows the TCP platform and the parked projects; system fonts (the page's CSP allows no font from outside); every text colour passes 4.5:1.

- The board's build line was a month old: `state.tsv` of the board folder belonged to the work user and the runner (the owner) could not replace it in the sticky folder, so every write failed silently and left a temporary file (thousands). The stale file and the leftovers are gone; `board_write` now removes its temporary file when the rename fails.

- The console page lives in the repository as `awb/tcp/web/portal.html`, a byte copy of the page live since 2026-10-17 (`/srv/awb-web/index.html`); the steering session takes the page over from the UI queue (the owner's word of 2026-11-07).

- `awb files tidy` carries out the bucket rows of a move plan (BK3, B-045, branch task/B-045; D-B3). New `awb files tidy --go PLAN [--only owner-bucket|lab-bucket] [--project CODE] [--dry-run]`, owner side only (refused as the work user, inside an assistant session and without a terminal), takes the `PLAN.tsv` of `awb files plan` or its folder when it is less than 24 hours old, reads its bucket rows by the names of the header (a row struck from the file is never acted on), prints the rows and bytes per bucket and action and each delete with key and size and asks for `yes` (`--dry-run` stops there). `move`, `unsorted` and `archive` copy, head both, compare size and md5 (size only for a multipart etag, said in the log), then delete the source; an existing target is skipped, never overwritten. `delete` only when the named keeper exists with the size of its row and the md5 of the object or for `stale-in` when the vault's record of the imports still holds key and md5; without a keeper it is refused. A row whose object is gone or changed size, whose path is `[withheld]` or whose key or target is out of the shape of the plan is skipped. The row log `<shared>/files/tidy-<stamp>/LOG.tsv` goes up to `reports/tidy/<stamp>/LOG.tsv` of the owner bucket (`bucket_layout.tidy_key`); one failed row never stops the rest, exit 1 when one failed. The key is the lab key of `bucket_tenant` of keys.conf, read from the password store at call time (the key service has no copy and no delete). README ("Tidying the buckets"), docs/commands-guide.md, COMMANDS.md and INTERFACES.md describe it. Tests: `tests/test_bk3_tidy.py`.

- Pooled Reserved pricing (B-044, branch task/B-044, KB-NPRJ). New `awb price pool SERVICE --flavor F --os OS --hours H [H ...] [--term R12|R24|R36|RU12|RU24|RU36 ...] [--start YYYY-MM-DD] [--region R] [--project DIR]` prices Reserved for many machines that each run part time: the record of one billing item (flavor plus OS; a choice that fits Windows and Linux is refused, KB-SFB4) is fetched live and for every term the unit count n is chosen once over the term from the pooled hours of all instances (the smaller n on a tie), in 730 h months or the calendar months from `--start` with the first and last month pro rata. Each term prints the best n, the monthly figures (units, covered and rest hours, Reserved, Open Elastic, total, Open Elastic only, saving), the term total against Open Elastic with the fetch time, the threshold R / PAYG, a month that alone would take another n and the notes: minimum order volume agreed with Telekom Sales (open point), five working days lead time, the billing item, expiring hours and for a GPU flavor the open conflict KB-NAAF. A term with the rate 0 is not offered (refused with exit 1 when asked for); an Upfront term uses RU as the monthly equivalent and says the whole total is invoiced at the start. Inside a project the monthly total, the term total and the saving are recorded with `awb calc`. The arithmetic is `awb/tcp/pricepool.py` (exact decimals, no I/O, command only, loaded by name through `deploy.DYNAMIC`). `awb pricebook` takes `mode: pool` with `pooled_hours:`, `term:` and optional `start:` and writes such an item as two lines, the Reserved units and the Open Elastic rest hours, with an assumption that names the pooled hours, the month model and the open minimum order volume. `awb price check` adds one note to a sheet with a reserved row (Reserved is a pooled contingent per billing item, use `awb price pool`) and a warning when the units of a reserved row equal the quantity of a PAYG row of the same record; neither changes a verdict. The skill tcp-price has a part "Reserved for many machines". README, docs/commands-guide.md and COMMANDS.md describe it. Tests: `tests/test_price_pool.py` on a fake fetch, no row replaced.

- Bucket rows of the files plan get actions and sizes (BK2, B-043, branch task/B-043; design of the buckets accepted 2026-11-06 with D-B1 archive 30 days after fetch, D-B2 no cold storage class, D-B3 delete only through a plan row the owner leaves in). `awb files plan` gives every object of the bucket inventories one row with its size (bytes) and one action: `keep` in its layout place (also an object of `in/` no take or import took yet, a put not fetched or fetched less than 30 days ago, an old `put-<hex>` key, a lab key outside the layout), `move` of a loose object of the owner bucket (at the top or in a folder not in the layout) of one project by its folder, its row or its identical copies to `<YYYY-MM>/<code>/out/<name>` when it is identical to a deliverable, else `in/<name>`, `unsorted` of a loose object without one project or month to `_unsorted/<stamp>/<its key>` (one already there stays), `archive` of a readable put whose INDEX.md row says fetched 30 days or more before the plan date (`ARCHIVE_DAYS`) to `<code>/_archive/<YYYY-MM of the put>/from-session/...` and of an older version in `out/` to `<YYYY-MM>/<code>/_archive/<stamp>/<name>`, `delete` only for an identical copy inside `out/` or `from-session/` of one project (the keeper named; these were `duplicate`) and an object of `in/` whose key and md5 the vault's record of a take or an import holds (reason `stale-in`; these were `stale-in` rows for every object of `in/`), `withheld` for a path that is withheld or reads as no key. The fetched dates come from `<code>/from-session/INDEX.md` through the key service (op obs GET, as `awb fetch` reads); one that cannot be read is said and its puts stay keep. `PLAN.tsv` gets the column `size` last, `PLAN.md` ends with rows and bytes per action per project and per bucket and a total line, the run line adds the bucket counts per action and the bytes of the delete rows. The plan still moves and deletes nothing. `awb clean files plan` on the laptop reads PLAN.tsv by the names of its header and refuses one without id, place, action, target or project; a laptop on an older release refuses the new header until it is updated. README, docs/commands-guide.md, COMMANDS.md and INTERFACES.md describe it. Tests: `tests/test_bk2_bucket_plan.py`; replaced by the owner's decision of 2026-11-07: `tests/test_r5_files_plan.py::test_r5_the_plan_files_and_their_format` (9 columns) and `::test_r5_host_and_buckets_are_listed_and_never_moved` (the BK2 actions).

- The seal part of B-042: `awb-platform-status.timer` runs `awb platform-status pull` as the work user every 15 minutes (`seal/work-jobs/`, WORK_JOBS of `seal/setup.sh` and `seal/verify.sh`, a verify line), and `status.otc-service.com` tcp 443 is the eighth allowed host of the egress filter (`seal/egress/ALLOW.md`, the owner's yes of 2026-11-07).

- The TCP platform on the board (B-042, branch task/B-042). `awb platform-status pull` reads the public T-Cloud Status Dashboard (`GET /v2/events` and `GET /v2/components` of `status.otc-service.com`, HTTPS, host fixed in the code, 20 s, at most 2 MB, no key) and replaces `<shared>/platform-status/state.tsv` atomically: a header row with the time of the pull and `ok` or the reason (`timeout`, `http`, `json`) and one row for each incident open now and each event that starts within 14 days. A failed pull keeps the last good rows. Every cell is matched against a fixed form when written and again when read. `awb board` (Markdown and HTML) gets a section "TCP platform" after the header lines (open incidents, planned maintenances by start, `status of <time>` or `status not pulled since <time>` after 2 hours, `no open incident`), `GET /api/board` the field `platform` (docs/api/openapi.yaml regenerated) and `awb platform-status show` prints the section alone. README, docs/commands-guide.md and COMMANDS.md describe it. The timer and the egress row for `status.otc-service.com` are the seal's and not in this change.
- Readable put keys, kind folders and INDEX.md (BK1, B-041, branch task/B-041; design of the buckets accepted 2026-11-06). `awb xchg put` lands at `<code>/from-session/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<kind>/<code>-<kind>-<lang>-<HHMM>.<ext>` instead of `<date>/put-<hex>.<ext>`: the kind word of `--kind` (`doc` without it), the language of `--lang` or the guess of the text, the UTC minute, `-2`, `-3` for a second put of the key in the same minute (never an overwrite, also not of the HTML page beside a Markdown put, which takes the same stem). `--as NAME` gives `<code>-<kind>-<stem of NAME>.<ext>` when the name check of the key service passes it, else the neutral key and one line without the name. The key service alone builds the key (a session sends kind, lang and the name, never a readable key), runs the name check on the `--as` name and on the whole key and falls back to `<code>-doc-<lang>-<HHMM>.<ext>` on a hit; `put_key_ok` takes the new form and the old `put-<hex>` one, so old keys stay valid for `awb xchg list`, `awb fetch` and `awb xchg clean`. `<code>/from-session/INDEX.md` (date, time, key, kind, lang, size, review, fetched; newest first) is rewritten by the key service on every put, after a delete and when `awb fetch` marks a key fetched (the new obs call FETCHED, the owner's processes only). `awb xchg list` shows kind and key, `awb fetch` writes a file under its readable name, the owner's mail names the readable key, the put line of a project's CLAUDE.md asks for `--kind WORD` and `--lang XX` (`awb projects sync` appends it). Four tests of the `put-<hex>` key replaced by `tests/test_bk1_put_keys.py`: `test_a_put_lands_under_its_id_and_tells_the_owner_by_the_id_alone`, `test_xchg_put_of_a_markdown_file_writes_the_html_beside_it_and_names_both`, `test_xchg_put_of_another_file_writes_no_page` and `test_gt_a_session_put_of_a_customer_deliverable_succeeds_and_replaces_the_secret`.

- WL `awb winlab list` in a project session (fix of 2026-11-06): the work user reads no registry of its own, so the list said "no lab machine registered" while winlab1 was registered and online. In a session it now asks the lab broker and shows the machines whose window is open for its own project with the end, or who opens one; other machines and other projects stay unseen. Tests: `tests/test_wl_session_list.py`.

- WL disk images get the secret check only, at transfer speed (B-040, branch task/B-040; live check of 2026-11-06: `check_file` took about 77 s per 4 MB of high-entropy data before a byte was sent, days for a 12 GB VM image). A file of `winlab-out/` whose name ends in .vhdx, .vhd, .vmdk, .qcow2 or .iso (any case) and whose bytes carry the signature of the format (`vhdxfile`, `conectix` at offset 0 or in the footer, `KDMV` or the text descriptor, `QFI\xfb`, `CD001` at 0x8001) is a disk image, so is a raw .img of whole sectors (`winlabsession.disk_image`). It never reaches the name check and still gets the secret check over all of its bytes in pieces with the overlap. That check (`_image_secret_count`) runs the same secret check over the lines that can carry a secret with the lines around them and over the whole piece again when they carry one, so it refuses what the check refused before, with the same count. About 0.5 s per 4 MB of random bytes, against 27 s. Every other file is checked as before, also one with such a name and no signature. The log line of a put names the check that ran. Tests: `tests/test_wl_image_check.py`, no row replaced.

- WL put_chunk of the lab broker over sftp (B-039, branch task/B-039; live check of 2026-11-06 on winlab1: Windows sshd stopped passing standard input to powershell.exe after about 2 KB, so every put above that hung until its timeout). `SshTransport.put_chunk` (`awb/tcp/winbroker.py`) writes the chunk to a file of mode 600 in the broker's private folder (removed in every case), puts it with `sftp -b -` to `PART.awb-chunk` next to the part with the options of ssh (the broker key, the pinned known_hosts, HostKeyAlias, BatchMode, the port, the account) and then one ssh run checks the offset, appends `PART.awb-chunk` to the part, deletes it and prints the size. The answer to the session is the same: the size or the offset refusal. The path goes to sftp as `/C:/dir/file`; a path that cannot be written that way is refused with one line, nothing sent. A `PART.awb-chunk` left by a broken call is overwritten by the next put and never appended twice. An sftp exit other than 0 reads as offline when ssh could not reach the machine and as failed otherwise. The script still goes on standard input, no data chunk does; get_chunk is unchanged. setup.ps1 already names `Subsystem sftp` with `AllowUsers` the lab account, so it needs no change. Tests: `tests/test_wl_broker_sftp.py` on a fake ssh and a fake sftp; the fake of `tests/test_wl_broker.py` now takes the chunk over sftp, no assertion changed.

- WL on the real laptop (2026-11-05): `awb/winlab/setup.ps1` gives the account a description of 36 characters (Windows takes 48 at most) and puts the first line of the sshd config in parentheses (the comma binds before `+`, so the whole config became one line). `tests/test_wl_scripts_windows.py` checks both rules on setup.ps1 and remove.ps1. The tests point `AWB_WINLAB_SOCKET` at a missing path, so no test reaches the live lab broker of the host.

- The secret scrub of `awb bucket put` (and of every text that runs `cleanlogs.wipe_secrets`) keeps a PowerShell cmdlet call after `=` as code: `$secret = New-Object System.Security.SecureString` stays as it is, a quoted value, a value after `:` and a word with no PowerShell verb are still replaced (`awb/labsecrets.py` code_value). The setup script of task WL reached the laptop broken by it on 2026-11-05. Tests: `tests/test_putcopy_cmdlet.py`.

- The seal parts of the lab broker (task WL, steering session): `seal/awb-winlab.service` (root, group awb, loads the table inet awb_winlab before each start) and `step_winlab` of `seal/setup.sh`, run in the first seal after step_ssh and alone with the new `seal/setup.sh --winlab` on a host sealed before it. It adds the system user `awb-winlab` without a shell (the account the laptops open their tunnel to), `/etc/awb/winlab/` with the broker's key `id_ed25519` (mode 600) and the root owned `authorized_keys` of the laptops (each line carries `restrict,port-forwarding,permitlisten="127.0.0.1:PORT"`), the sshd drop-in `awb-winlab.conf` (key only, remote forwarding on loopback only, no session, no TTY; checked with `sshd -t` before the reload, removed when refused) and the nft table that drops the ports 2200-2299 and every registered port for the users awb, awb-build and awb-ask. A deploy leaves the step out, as it leaves out step_ssh.

- The lab broker, the root owned connector to the owner's Windows lab laptop (task WL part 4 of 4, B-038, branch task/B-038): new `awb winlab serve [--socket PATH] [--key PATH]` (`awb/tcp/winbroker.py`), the real transport of part 3. It holds the host's private key for the lab machines (default `/etc/awb/winlab/id_ed25519`, mode 600, refused otherwise), serves the session commands on a Unix socket (default `/run/awb-winlab/broker.sock`, mode 660) and before every call refuses with one line, nothing sent: a call without the key token of its project folder, an unregistered machine, no window, an expired window, another project and a host key that does not match the pinned fingerprint (ssh-keyscan, then ssh with `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes` against that key alone). Each call is one ssh connection to 127.0.0.1:PORT; powershell.exe reads the script and the bytes of a chunk from standard input. `winlabsession.connect` now gives the transport over the broker; a session without a registry of its own asks the broker for the window and the machine. `awb owner status` lists every registered lab machine that is online, `awb winlab add` takes `--account` for a laptop account other than `awblab`. The seal parts (the user `awb-winlab`, the sshd Match block, the unit, the nftables rule) come from the steering session. Tests: `tests/test_wl_broker.py` on a fake ssh, no row replaced.

- Commands, jobs and files of a project session on a Windows lab machine (task WL part 3 of 4, B-037, branch task/B-037), over a transport interface (`run`, `put_chunk`, `get_chunk`, `stat`) whose SSH implementation comes with part 4; until then the commands refuse with "no transport". New `awb winlab run NAME [--vm VM] [--timeout 300] -- POWERSHELL`, `awb winlab start NAME [--vm VM] -- POWERSHELL` (a scheduled task of the laptop's account with its log on the laptop; prints a job id), `awb winlab status|log|stop JOB` (running, ended with its exit code, stopped, lost or machine offline; log fetches the new part of the job's log), `awb winlab put FILE WINPATH` and `awb winlab get WINPATH FILE` (awb/winlabsession.py). Each first asks `winlab.allowed(NAME, CODE)`. A command goes out only without a `{{secret:NAME}}` reference, a name of the machine's own state, a registered name and a secret; `--vm VM` runs it in the VM with PowerShell Direct and the laptop reads the guest credential from `%ProgramData%\awb-winlab\vm\<VM>.txt` (Administrators and SYSTEM only), so no password passes through the session. CLIXML progress records are stripped, output lands by the rules of `awb paste --own` (new `paste.own_text`) and every command, output, job and transfer is logged in `notes/winlab/` of the project. put takes regular files of `winlab-out/` only, after the name check and the secret check; it refuses `input/`, the vault and the shared folders; get writes into the project, never into `input/` or `.git`. Both go in chunks, resume after a break, compare the SHA-256 at the end, never overwrite and are built for 12 GB. `remove.ps1` now also removes the job tasks (step job-tasks). Tests: `tests/test_wl_session.py` on a fake transport, no row replaced.

- The setup and removal scripts for the owner's Windows lab laptop (task WL part 2 of 4, B-036, branch task/B-036). New `awb winlab bootstrap NAME --host-key PUB --tunnel USER@HOST [--port PORT] [--account awblab] [--server-key PUB] [--ssh-port 22]` prints `setup.ps1` (the template `awb/winlab/setup.ps1`, filled; refused for the work user and in an assistant session; it writes nothing), which the owner runs once on the laptop as administrator: the OpenSSH server on 127.0.0.1 port 22 only (stopped before its first start with the new config, the listen addresses read back after the start), a local account (default `awblab`) in Administrators and Hyper-V Administrators with a random password nobody keeps and key logon only, the host's key in `administrators_authorized_keys` (inheritance off, Administrators and SYSTEM), the laptop's own tunnel key and the visible scheduled task `awb-winlab-tunnel` that holds `ssh -N -R 127.0.0.1:PORT:127.0.0.1:22 USER@HOST` from boot on (ServerAliveInterval 30, ServerAliveCountMax 3, ExitOnForwardFailure yes, the host's server key pinned) and starts it again 30 seconds after it ends. Every step checks its own result and stops with one line naming the step; at the end it prints the host key fingerprint for `awb winlab add` and the tunnel public key. `awb winlab remove NAME` now also prints `remove.ps1` (stdout, the registry line on stderr; `--script-only` keeps the machine registered), which removes the task, the service, the keys, the account and its profile folder and `%ProgramData%\awb-winlab`, guided by the `state.txt` setup leaves there so it takes nothing setup did not make. `awb deploy` ships `awb/winlab/` with the release as package data whose change restarts no unit (`deploy.PACKAGE_DATA`). Tests: `tests/test_wl_scripts.py` (the scripts checked as text, nothing runs PowerShell), no row replaced.

- The registry of the owner's Windows lab machines and their windows (task WL part 1 of 4, B-035, branch task/B-035): the bookkeeping on the owner's side for a later part that lets a project session run PowerShell on his Windows lab laptop (Hyper-V, an evaluation VM, no customer data); no network, no SSH and no PowerShell here. New `awb winlab add NAME --port PORT --fingerprint SHA256:... --no-customer-data`, `awb winlab open NAME --minutes N --project tcp-xxxx`, `awb winlab close NAME` and `awb winlab remove NAME` (owner side only: refused for the work user and in an assistant session, as `awb tenant mode` is) and `awb winlab list` for anyone (awb/winlab.py). A machine has a name, a loopback port from 1024 to 65535 (one machine per port), the pinned fingerprint of its host key (`SHA256:` and 43 base64 characters), the day of its registration and the owner's statement that it holds no customer data; add refuses a machine without `--no-customer-data`. A window gives one project one machine for 1 to 1440 minutes and ends by itself at its end, with nothing running for it; a machine has at most one open window. The registry is `winlab.json` beside keys.conf, mode 600, written to a fresh name and renamed; a registry that does not read allows nothing. `winlab.allowed(NAME, CODE)` is the one check of the later parts: it returns the machine or refuses with one line (an unregistered machine, no window, an expired window, another project). `awb owner status` lists every open window with its project, its end and `awb winlab close NAME`. Tests: `tests/test_wl_registry.py`, no row replaced.

- One shared PDF writer and `awb pdf FILE [--out PDF] [--title T] [--replace]` for every project (task PDF, B-033, branch task/B-033; on 2026-11-04 a project session began to write its own PDF builder because the host has no office suite). The standard-library writer of the dataset moved into the core module `awb/pdf.py` and became general: A4, the four standard fonts, word wrap, page breaks, headings h1 to h3, paragraphs, bullet and numbered lists, code blocks in Courier that keep their lines and indents (a long line wraps with a drawn arrow, never cut and never copied), tables as aligned Courier text, links with their URL and a link annotation, page numbers, title and author in the document info. It reads the Workbench's Markdown and the HTML of `awb howto render`. The PDF goes next to FILE or to `--out`, inside the project folder or the session scratch folder only; a character outside WinAnsi (Cyrillic for example) refuses it with its line and the count unless `--replace` writes `?`. `awb howto render --pdf` now writes the PDF (it said "no PDF yet" and exited 1) and the dataset's HOW-TO.pdf is made by the same writer, with the same text and a page number added.

- Write mode of a customer tenant without a separate write credential (TW2): when pass holds nothing under `awb/tenant/<alias>/write/`, `awb tenant mode ALIAS write` and the key service use the tenant's registered key or login (`ak` and `sk`, or `login/domain`, `user` and `password`, as the readonly line names it), so a tenant registered with an ordinary key or login of the customer's admin and granted before task TW gets write mode with the mode command alone. A whole write key or write login is used as before; a partial one, or both, stays refused with the line that names what is missing. The mode command and the tenant summary of `awb keys unlock` name the credential write mode uses (write key, write login, registered key or registered login) by its entries, never a value. A registered login takes its token as the read path does and signs every method. Read-only mode is unchanged. Two tests of the old rule replaced: `test_tw_write_mode_without_the_write_credential_is_refused_with_one_line` (now `..._signs_with_the_registered_key`) and `test_tw_the_mode_command_refuses_write_without_the_write_credential_in_pass` (now `..._refuses_a_partial_write_credential_or_none_at_all`).

- A customer tenant reaches the customer's buckets under their own names (TW3, the owner's decision of 2026-11-04): on a tenant of the readonly line `awb cloud call` reads any bucket and, in write mode, writes any bucket, and `awb lab obs` on a customer tenant in write mode takes any bucket; the project prefix `<code>-...` stays on the shared lab tenants test-10491 and test-10497, the exchange buckets stay with awb xchg and IAM stays out of `awb cloud call`. Two tests of the old rule replaced: `test_tw_write_mode_keeps_iam_and_the_obs_buckets_of_other_projects_out` (now `..._keeps_iam_out_and_reaches_the_customer_buckets`) and the customer case of `test_lr_test_10498_and_a_customer_tenant_are_refused` (the read-only line of TW).

- One switch per customer tenant between read-only and write (task TW, B-031, branch task/B-031; route B of B-030, a write window on a customer tenant once the customer gave a written OK). New `awb tenant mode ALIAS write|readonly [--note TEXT]`, owner side only (refused for the work user and in an assistant session): write checks by name that pass holds the write credential beside the read one (`awb/tenant/<alias>/write/ak` and `write/sk`, or `write/login/domain`, `user`, `password`; without it one line names what is missing), keeps the day and the note in the new `tenant_write` line of keys.conf (awb/tenantmode.py) and runs `awb keys unlock`; a tenant the line does not name stays read-only. In write mode the key service signs every method for the projects granted the tenant with the write credential, which it holds only in write mode: `awb cloud call` (a write needs an active project, an OBS write a bucket of the project, IAM only in a lab run) and lab runs with `awb lab obs` (labadmin and labobs take the write key or the write login in place of an admin login), with the same run log and no key in it; no lease and no new egress. The end of a run on a customer tenant deletes only what the run made (no record of a PUT or PATCH outside OBS, a server's delete keeps its elastic IP and data disks). readonly reaches the running key service at once through the new admin op `tenant_readonly`: the write credential is forgotten, the next write is refused and an open lab obs job of the tenant is aborted with the reason in its state; an unlock that drops the mode does the same. `awb owner status` lists every tenant in write mode with its day and note, no expiry; `awb tenant list` shows the mode of each customer tenant. Tests: `tests/test_tw_tenant_write.py` on stand-ins without a socket to 127.0.0.1, no row replaced.

- Large objects to and from OBS in a lab run (task LO, B-030, branch task/B-030; found on 2026-11-03 with a 10 GB VHDX on test-10491 in tcp-bytg, where an IMS image import stopped at the upload). New `awb lab obs put FILE obs://BUCKET/KEY [--tenant T]`, `awb lab obs get obs://BUCKET/KEY FILE [--tenant T]`, `awb lab obs status [JOB]` and `awb lab obs abort JOB`; `labclient.obs_put`, `obs_get`, `obs_status`, `obs_abort` and `obs_wait` for the scripts. The new op `lab_obs` of the call socket (awb/tcp/labobs.py) has the key service move the bytes as a background job under the rules of a lab call (the open run of the project on test-10491 or test-10497, never a read-only tenant, a bucket `<code>-...`, never an exchange bucket, the run's temporary credential, signed like a lab call); the session holds no key and gets no new egress. The file comes as an open descriptor over the socket (SCM_RIGHTS) with one of the run log, never as a path; a get writes only inside the project folder or `/tmp/claude-<uid>` and checks the free disk first. A put hashes the file, sends a multipart upload (64 MiB parts, larger within 10000 parts, 128 GB in 1908) with each part tried again after a broken connection and checks size and `x-obs-meta-sha256` at the end; the same put or get once more resumes an interrupted job. The records are `<vault>/lab-runs/obs/<job>.json`; a lock, a stop or a reload of the key service saves a job as interrupted, never lost. The run log gets bucket, key, size, hash, duration and state. keys.conf takes `lab_obs_max_gb` (default 200). `awb lab run end` stops the run's jobs and aborts the open multipart uploads of its buckets before the deletes (409 BucketNotEmpty otherwise). `labadmin.MAX_BODY` and the egress list are unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_lo_lab_obs.py` on the in-memory stand-in `FakeObsLab` of tests/lab_fake.py, no row replaced.

### Fixed

- A rerun of `awb dataset weekly` on the same day refused itself (task DW2, branch dw2-weekly-rerun): the run of 2026-11-02 had put the bucket copy before it failed at GitHub, and the rerun stopped with `the bucket holds the dataset of 2026-11-02 already; give --replace`. The weekly publish now replaces that day's copy in the bucket and on GitHub, as a later build of the same day always did. `awb dataset put` by hand still refuses a held day without `--replace`. Tests: `tests/test_dw2_weekly_rerun.py`, no row replaced.

- The weekly publish of the dataset TCP Facts failed on 2026-11-02 with `awb dataset weekly: failed, put: Error (operating system error)` (task DW, branch dw-setgid-copy). The build folders under the shared side carry setgid and the owner's unit `awb-dataset-weekly` runs with RestrictSUIDSGID, so `shutil.copytree`, which copies the mode of a folder, got EPERM when it copied `topics/` into the clone. `github_put` now copies a folder with `copy_plain_tree`: a folder gets the default mode, a file its read and write bits. Tests: `tests/test_dw_setgid_copy.py`, no row replaced.

## Week 44, 2026-10-26 to 2026-11-01
### Added

- The weekly fact recheck reads its service project from the host file (task RP, branch rp-recheck-project). The run of 2026-11-01 stopped with `is not an active project` because the code was written in the runtime and the owner had closed and deleted that project. `awb kb recheck-run` and `awb kb recheck-watch` now take `--project`, else `recheck_project` of the host file (read like `recheck_tenant`; the work user reads the root file alone); with neither the run stops with one line that names the key. `seal/setup.sh` keeps the key when it rewrites the file and `seal/verify.sh` warns without it. `awb close` and `awb projects delete` refuse the project that key names (close unless `--force`, delete unless `--service`), and `awb projects list` marks it. Tests: `tests/test_rp_service_project.py`, no row replaced.

- Two fixes of the laptop side found on his work laptop on 2026-10-30 (tasks ZS and FA, branch zs-fa-laptop). ZS: oh-my-zsh replaces `~/.zshrc` on install and the PATH and proxy lines added there were lost (`awb` not found, then the object storage not reached). New `awb clean shell` writes the PATH line of `~/.local/bin` and the source line of `~/.config/winrelay.sh` as a marked block into `~/.bashrc` and, when `~/.oh-my-zsh` exists, into `${ZSH_CUSTOM:-$HOME/.oh-my-zsh/custom}/awb.zsh`, both idempotent, `~/.zshrc` never touched; `awb init --laptop` and `awb clean init` name it while a line is missing; `awb clean doctor` names the fix of a missing `awb.zsh` under zsh with oh-my-zsh and of an unset `HTTPS_PROXY` while `winrelay.sh` exists. FA: `awb clean fetch --again NAME` (repeatable) fetches a file of `out/` again although `fetched.tsv` records it and keeps a local file of that name (the new one gets ` (2)`). Tests: `tests/test_zs_fa_laptop.py`, no row replaced.

- The move plan carried out on the laptop (task R6 of presentations/reports-design, branch r6-clean-files, sections 3.3 to 3.5, his yes of 2026-10-29 to the layout and the defaults). New `awb clean layout [ROOT] [--save]` sets the layout root once (`<root>/YYYY-MM/tcp-xxxx/private|clean|out|_archive/`); `awb clean use tcp-xxxx [--month YYYY-MM]` then makes the project folders there and takes `clean/` as the work folder, `awb clean fetch` without `--to` lands in `out/`. New `awb clean files plan [STAMP|latest]` (awb/cleanfiles.py) fetches `reports/plan/<stamp>/PLAN.tsv` with the laptop key and prints it with the real paths of the local inventory, on the screen only; `awb clean files apply [STAMP] [--go] [--include-onedrive]` is a dry run without `--go` and with it moves the files (never a delete, never an overwrite, the sha256 of each file checked against the inventory, the report folder, the report workbook, OneDrive and anything outside the inventory roots and the layout root refused) and writes the undo journal `~/awb-laptop/vault/moves/<stamp>.tsv`; `awb clean files undo [STAMP]` moves everything back. Tests: `tests/test_r6_clean_files.py`, no row replaced.

- The move plan of the project files (task R5 of presentations/reports-design, branch r5-files-plan, sections 3.2 to 3.5, his yes of 2026-10-29 to the layout and the defaults). New `awb files plan [--laptop FILE|NAME|latest]... [--host FILE] [--buckets FILE...] [--put]` (awb/filesplan.py) reads the newest laptop inventory of `reports/inventory/` through the key service and the newest host and bucket inventories of `~/tcp-shared/files/`, checks the laptop rows again at entry, matches identical files by hash, probable ones by size and time and versions by group and kind, and writes `~/tcp-shared/files/plan-<stamp>/PLAN.md` and `PLAN.tsv` (id, place, action, target, project, path, keeper, reason): laptop `keep`, `move` into `YYYY-MM/tcp-xxxx/private|clean|out/` and `archive` into `YYYY-MM/tcp-xxxx/_archive/<stamp>/`; host and buckets `stale-in`, `duplicate` and `withheld`, listed only; nothing deleted; ids and coded paths only. `--put` sends the laptop rows to `reports/plan/<stamp>/PLAN.tsv` of the owner bucket for the laptop step R6; the key service's owner put takes that key and nothing else under `reports/plan/` (`bucket_layout.plan_key`). Tests: `tests/test_r5_files_plan.py`, no row replaced.

- The file inventories of the host and the buckets (task R4 of presentations/reports-design, branch r4-files-inventory, his yes of 2026-10-29 to the layout and the defaults). New `awb files inventory --host|--buckets [--out FILE]` (awb/filesinv.py): `--host` walks the project folders of the register as the work user (`sudo -n -u awb -H`), hash only, no content read; `--buckets` lists the owner bucket `awb` (project folders of live projects and `inbox/`, `reports/` and the dataset skipped) and the lab bucket through the key service, the etag as the md5 of a single-part object. The rows are those of the shared module awb/inventory.py of task R3 (one file per place: `host-<stamp>.tsv`, `owner-bucket-<stamp>.tsv`, `lab-bucket-<stamp>.tsv`; `--out FILE` takes all rows of the run). Every path passes the check of a text that leaves the host, a hit is `[withheld]`; a check that cannot run writes nothing. The key service has the new owner op `lab_list` on the admin socket (the top with "/", `inbox/` and `<code>/` of the lab bucket, list only) its owner `bucket_list` takes `inbox/` and the owner's CLI may read the laptop inventories `reports/inventory/<place>-<stamp>[-<label>].tsv` (`bucket_get`) for the plan of R5. Default place `~/tcp-shared/files/`. Tests: `tests/test_r4_files_inventory.py`, no row replaced.

- The laptop lists the files of the folders he names for the move plan (task R3, branch r3-clean-inventory, presentations/reports-design/DESIGN.md §3.1, his yes to the layout and the defaults of §7). New `awb clean inventory ROOT... [--project tcp-xxxx] [--label LABEL] [--put] [--include-onedrive]` (awb/cleaninventory.py) walks only the named roots, opens a file only to hash it and keeps the full list with the real paths in `~/awb-laptop/vault/inventory/<stamp>.tsv` (600, laptop only); the coded copy `~/awb-laptop/inventory/laptop-<stamp>.tsv` carries every path segment through the name machinery of `awb clean` and `[withheld]` for a path that still carries something. Skipped: the report folder, the report workbook, the laptop folder, `_archive`, `.git`, `~$` and `.tmp` files, `Thumbs.db`, `desktop.ini` and links; a root under OneDrive needs `--include-onedrive`. `--put` sends the coded copy alone to `reports/inventory/` of the bucket with the laptop key, the path of `awb clean put`. The row format of the three inventories (the eleven columns, their order, the file name and the header) is the shared module awb/inventory.py, for `awb files inventory` too; `bucket_layout.INVENTORY_PREFIX` names the bucket place. Tests: `tests/test_r3_clean_inventory.py`, no row replaced.

- The codes file of the report workbook reaches the laptop and the report workbook never leaves it (task R2, branch r2-clean-report, presentations/reports-design/DESIGN.md, his choice of 2026-10-29: a separate codes file the workbook links to). New `awb clean report [--dir DIR] [--save]` (awb/cleanreport.py) downloads `reports/projects.csv` and `reports/projects.meta.json` of the owner bucket with the laptop's own key through the proxy, the path of `awb clean fetch` (no new credential), checks the sha256 of the sidecar, keeps the file before as `projects.previous.csv` and replaces `projects.csv` in one step with three tries two seconds apart while Excel holds it; the folder is saved in `~/awb-laptop/report-dir`; it never uploads. The fingerprint of the report workbook is a workbook with both sheets Names and Projects in `xl/workbook.xml`, whatever its suffix (awb/reportbook.py). `awb clean FILE`, `--put`, `awb clean put` and the page of `awb clean serve` refuse a file in the report folder (the saved one or any folder named `awb-report`) and a workbook with the fingerprint with the line `the report workbook never leaves the laptop`; `awb clean fetch --to` refuses the report folder. `awb import` and `awb import --auto` hold such a workbook: nothing of it is imported, it stays in `in/` and the vault inbox, one line `file N of M held: ...` without its name and one mail per file to delete it from `in/`. The rule with its reason and its walls is `rules/report-workbook.txt`. Tests: `tests/test_r2_clean_report.py`, no row replaced.

- The codes file of the report workbook (task R1 of presentations/reports-design, branch r1-report-codes, his choice of 2026-10-29: the workbook lives on the laptop and links to a separate codes file). New `awb report --codes [--out FILE] [--put] [--if-changed]` (awb/reportcodes.py, command only in awb/deploy.py) writes `projects.csv`: one row per project of the register, the six columns `project code`, `customer code`, `task description`, `next step`, `whose move`, `date of last change` (a test pins the names), UTF-8 with a byte order mark, CRLF, comma, every field quoted, ISO dates, sorted by project code, `none` for no customer, whose move `owner`, `session`, `customer` (parked) or `closed` (30 days after the last change); both texts pass the name check, the gate detectors and the candidate rule again and a hit is written as `(withheld by the name check)`; a check that cannot run writes nothing. Beside it `projects.meta.json` with the keys `written` (UTC, ISO 8601), `rows`, `withheld`, `sha256` (of the exact bytes of projects.csv) and `release`; a test pins `sha256` and `written`, which the laptop side reads. Default place `/var/lib/awb-report-codes/`. `--put` sends both to `reports/` of the owner bucket through the key service, which allows those two keys there and nothing else (awb/bucket_layout.py REPORT_KEYS); a failed put is an item `reports: ...` of `awb owner status` until a put succeeds. The owner job `awb-report-codes` (seal/work-jobs, hourly at minute 50, `--put --if-changed`) is installed by the seal and by `setup.sh --update` (new list OWNER_JOBS) and proved by `verify.sh`. `status_of`, `_git_dates` and `withhold` of awb/period.py moved into awb/projectfacts.py; `awb report --period` is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_r1_report_codes.py`, no row replaced.

- The dataset TCP Facts is published once a week by itself (task KS, branch ks-fact-schedule, his decision of 2026-10-28). New `awb dataset weekly` runs `awb dataset build` of today, `awb kb review` of that folder (his own mark; the refusal for the work user stays) and `awb dataset put --github`, each step only after the one before succeeded; the put keeps its checks (the review mark, the two README statements, no price data, the person scan). `seal/setup.sh` writes the owner unit `awb-dataset-weekly.service` and its timer (Monday 06:00 UTC, 07:00 Berlin in winter, a missed run made up at boot) in the new function dataset_weekly_unit and enables the timer; `seal/verify.sh` proves it enabled with the unit running `awb dataset weekly` as the owner. The result goes to `/var/lib/awb-dataset-weekly/last.json`: a failure is the item `dataset: weekly publish failed: <step>: <reason>` of `awb owner status` until a publish succeeds, a success the line `dataset tcp-facts-<date>: N added, M amended, K retired` of the 08:00 mail of the next 24 hours (sent for that line even when nothing waits on him). Tests: `tests/test_ks_fact_schedule.py`.

- A fact leaves the dataset TCP Facts only when something invalidates it, never for its date (task KS, his decision of 2026-10-28; tcp-facts-2026-10-28 had dropped 104 availability facts checked 2026-09-24 and 25 only because their 30 days had passed). `awb dataset build` keeps an expired fact with `"recheck_due": true` in facts.jsonl, `recheck due` after its check date in facts.md and topics/, a row of the new README table "Facts with a recheck due" and the count `recheck_due` in MANIFEST.json. It leaves out a retired fact (a superseded one is retired for its successor), one a recheck refuted on or after its check date (the new `~/tcp-shared/recheck/refuted.tsv`, one line per refuted verdict, counted as "contradicted by a recheck") and, as before, one that names a service the service description no longer offers. `awb kb export` is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_ks_fact_schedule.py`, no row replaced.

- Knowledge base first (task KF, branch kf-kb-first; his decision of 2026-10-28: a session spent a 35-minute live run on an OBS 403 while facts of other projects already said that IAM-granted OBS rights take 20 to 30 minutes and a bucket policy works in seconds). The prompt hook adds "Known from the knowledge base (N facts on this topic; ...)" with one line per fact (id, grade, check date, statement) when a prompt starts a new task: the first prompt of a session, a prompt after more than two hours of silence or one that names a run, a customer question or a deliverable whose closest facts were not told in the session yet (awb/kbfirst.py). The facts are those of `kb.find` for the prompt, then for the goal and tags of SCOPE.md, each sharing at least two content words with the query, screened like `awb kb find`, at most ten and 1500 characters. A base that cannot be read adds nothing and never blocks; the client's agent and CI events get no block. The project CLAUDE.md start line gains "Before a new task or a run: read the knowledge base facts the hook shows ..." (`awb projects sync` adds it once) and the work rules a "Knowledge base first" line. `awb review claims` writes the column `known_before` (yes/no) into kb.tsv and prints how many claims were known before the work; the final check asks for the same yes/no column for each caveat and claim. Tests: `tests/test_kf_kb_first.py`; tests_replaced: the header assertion of `test_the_claims_get_their_knowledge_entries_and_one_can_settle_a_claim` (kb.tsv gained a column; successor `test_the_review_claims_say_which_claim_was_known_before`).

- Lab runs with the admin logins of the two lab tenants (task LR, branch lr-lab-runs, his decision of 2026-10-28: a project session tests its own scripts on test-10491 and test-10497 on his word, without asking him for a login). keys.conf takes `lab_admin = test-10491 test-10497` (no other alias); `awb keys unlock` loads `awb/admin/<alias>/domain`, `user` and `password` from pass. New ops `lab_run` and `lab_call` of the call socket (awb/tcp/labadmin.py): any method on any service, IAM, STS and OBS included, only inside an open run of the calling project; the service takes and caches the tokens, answers in full and shortens tokens, temporary keys and security tokens (awb/labsecrets.py); passwords and logins never appear. `awb lab run start|end|status` and `from awb import labclient` (awb/labclient.py, command only, core code that speaks the socket itself) for the scripts; the run records in `<vault>/lab-runs/` (awb/labruns.py); every create recorded with its delete, the end deletes in reverse; the call log in `notes/lab-runs/<run>.log`; old or failed runs in `awb owner status`. The work rules and the project template carry the lab run rule (`awb projects sync` adds it). Tests: `tests/test_lr_lab_runs.py`, no row replaced.

- A read-only tenant reads every project of its domain the login can see (task T2, branch t2-readonly-projects, reported by a project session: the sub-projects of eu-de and eu-nl were not readable). `awb tenant add-readonly` writes eu-de and eu-nl when `--region` names none (the keys.conf line `ALIAS:login:DOMAIN-ID:eu-de+eu-nl`; an old line of one region still works). `awb tenant regions ALIAS --region R [--region R]...` changes the regions of a registered one without asking for its login and runs `awb keys unlock`. `awb cloud call ... --tenant ALIAS --scope NAME|ID` names a region project or a sub-project (eu-de_<suffix>): the key service checks it against GET /v3/auth/projects (kept 5 minutes), refuses a project of another domain, an unknown one and one of a region the tenant does not read, takes a token scoped to that project by its id (one per project, in memory, renewed as before) and calls the endpoints of its region; a read key sends it as X-Project-Id. `awb tenant projects ALIAS` (the new op `projects` of the call socket) lists name, region and id of what a session can read. GET and HEAD only and the grant check of a call are unchanged; a scope on OBS is refused. Tests: `tests/test_t2_readonly_projects.py`, no row replaced.

- The laptop flow of `awb clean` with the project named once and a local page (tasks LC4 and LC5, branch lc45-clean-flow; the owner typed the project code four times and the file path twice for one file of a new project). `awb clean use tcp-xxxx` keeps the current project of the laptop and makes `~/work/tcp-xxxx` (`AWB_WORK_HOME`); `awb clean`, `awb clean put` and `awb clean fetch` take `--project` first, then the work folder of the current directory, then that project, and print `project: tcp-xxxx (from ...), customer CUST-XXXX` first. `--project` and `--kind` are no longer required: the kind is logs for a `.log` file and docs for any other (one run and one report per kind, the kind printed), the copies go into `clean/` of the work folder without `--project` and into `./clean/` with it, as before. A customer not registered on the laptop is asked for once on the terminal (no terminal: the old refusal). `awb clean put` sends the clean copies listed in the reports of the last run (`~/awb-laptop/last-run/tcp-xxxx`) without cleaning again; `--put` on the run is unchanged. A Windows path (`D:\Documents\x.docx`, `D:/Documents/x.docx`) is read as `/mnt/d/...`. `awb clean serve [--port 8765]` (new module awb/cleanserve.py, command-only in awb/deploy.py) is the same flow as one page of the standard library's http.server on 127.0.0.1: a random token per start in the printed URL that every request must carry, a Host header other than localhost or 127.0.0.1 refused, upload or typed paths, run, the report and a text view of each copy, put with the key of the serve process or the line how to load it; uploads in a temporary folder of mode 700 deleted after the run, no content logged. The guide has a section "Laptop flow" with the before and after. Tests: `tests/test_lc45_clean_flow.py`, no row replaced.

- The final check (task FX, branch fx-final-check): a last pass on a strong model for a high-stakes deliverable, from the one-off run of 2026-10-27 that found the lost caveats, a request dropped by contract.md, claims stronger than the evidence and answers out of the customer's order. `workflows/final-check.js` runs one agent pinned to Fable 5.1 (`model: 'fable'`) at effort max over input/, SCOPE.md, STATE.md, OPEN.md, evidence/, the review folder and the draft; it writes `deliverables/STEM.final.md` (the answer written again from the request) and `reviews/FILE/final-check.md` (the table); the script records the run in `reviews/FILE/final-check.json`. The tool guard passes it by its scriptPath like review.js and request-check.js (PD3), nothing else widened. The project template (awb/projects.py) has a section "Final check (FX)": his phrase "final check" or "финальная вычитка" starts it after one line on the cost and one word of his, never on its own; `awb projects sync` appends it to an older project. New `awb review show deliverables/FILE` lists the record state and the final check. `awb bucket put` does not ask for it. Tests: `tests/test_fx_final_check.py`, no row replaced.

- The final check stands in for the request check and the blind verification of its final text (task RV2, branch rv-review-fx, his decision of 2026-10-27). final-check.js asks its agent for the `sha256sum` of the final text and records it as `final_sha256` in final-check.json. While `deliverables/STEM.final.md` has that hash, its review runs `workflows/review.js` with `after_fx: true` (the platform lenses and the partner lens, no blind verification and no verify.json), `awb review pass` accepts it and records `final_check`. The send gate of `awb bucket put` takes the final check for the request check. After an edit or with a record without the hash the full review applies again and the pass and the gate say so. A final text is name-checked under the name of its draft, because the name check reads x.final.md as a web address: before, `awb review init` refused every final text ("url 1"). The template line of FX names the after_fx review; `awb projects sync` adds it to an older project. Tests: `tests/test_rv_review_fx.py`.

- `awb paste --own [--as NAME]` for our own output (task P1, branch p1-paste-own, the owner's rule of 2026-10-27: customer material is checked once, on the way in). A PoC script, a lab run on our own tenant or a key service answer gets only the secret finding of the gate (classes secret, private-key, token): each value becomes `[secret]` in place and the rest of the line stays; no name check, no structured pattern (phone, IBAN, MAC, mail, address, id), no refusal. It prints `own output: N secrets replaced` and the file, in `notes/pastes/` as before. A refused paste of the default mode is no longer lost: the raw text is kept 0600 in the pasting user's own `$XDG_STATE_HOME/awb/refused-pastes/` (default `~/.local/state`, the folder 0700, never in a project, never printed), the refusal names `awb paste --retry STAMP` and `awb paste --own --retry STAMP`, a written paste deletes the kept file and a kept file older than 7 days goes at the next paste. A `--as` name taken today is said in one line and written as `<NAME>-2` instead of a refusal. The project CLAUDE.md template and `awb projects sync` carry one new line: a script's output goes to a file first and then into `awb paste --own`, never a script piped straight into `awb paste`. Tests: `tests/test_p1_paste_own.py`; row T8-5 moves to its successor (a taken name now gets a number).


- Deliverables as HTML and md and notes from development (task DN, branch dn-notes-html; his rules of 2026-10-27: a link to a file on the host or in the bucket is useless to him, a text for a customer must paste into a mail, he relays nothing between sessions). `awb bucket put CODE FILE.md` writes `FILE.md` and `FILE.html` into out/: a small renderer in awb/bucket.py (`md_to_html`) renders headings, paragraphs, bold, italic, code, links as text, bullet and numbered lists, tables with borders and code blocks into one page with one inline style block, no asset and no script; the send gate is unchanged and judges the md.
